# @vector build progress

The build contract is [vector-at-mention-spec.md](./vector-at-mention-spec.md). Read [vector-at-mention-deviations.md](./vector-at-mention-deviations.md) before continuing. The build ran on branch `vector-at-mention` (cut from `main` at `78526052`), one work package at a time in the contract's order, then on `build-integrate` and `v2-vector`, which PR #33 (`release-finish`) joined into the combined v2 integration. The runtime and Brain transport packages continue on branch `brain-transport`, cut from `origin/release-finish` at `0ee486e3`; push only that branch (`git push origin HEAD:brain-transport`). Never push or merge to `main`. #33 is merged (`b362fdf6`; `origin/main` contains `0f034957`); the lead merges `origin/main` into this branch and opens its pull request.

For each package, record its commit, the focused tests and typechecks run locally with their results, and the CI runs actually seen. Never record a CI result that was not seen.

## Completed and current work

### V00. CI lane and test wiring

- **Status:** done. The lane is green on the V00 commit with no product change, and the three web GitHub API tests ran in CI (contract V00 "Done").
- **Commit:** `4ed48e7a` (`test(github): V00 add the @vector CI lane and test wiring`), pushed to `origin/vector-at-mention`. Origin accepted the push with the global pre-push hook (`/home/user/.hooks/pre-push`, the Bun-version check and then the turbo typecheck one package at a time) in place; the hook's output was not kept, so no typecheck result is recorded for it.
- **Files:** `.github/workflows/vector-at-mention.yml` (new); `packages/cloud/src/workflow-compliance.test.ts` (a `@vector CI lane` block placed after the release supply-chain block, with no new imports and no change to shared lists, so Codium's A00 edits to the same file merge cleanly); the deviations file (V00 entry). `packages/engine/src/cli/vector-account.ts` is untouched.
- **The lane:** pushes to `vector-at-mention` (no path filter), pull requests on `api/**`, `bun.lock`, the root `package.json`, the contract's other paths and the lane's tests (deviation 4), and dispatch (which works only once the file is on `main`). `contents: read`, no job permissions or environments, no repository secret, checkouts without persisted credentials. Every push and dispatch gets its own concurrency group, so a newer push never cancels or drops an older push's run; pull requests cancel stale runs. Jobs: `api` (hosted API typecheck; web GitHub tests against Valkey pinned by `vector-free-models.yml`'s digest; root `api-module-loading` and `cli-token`; the lane policy), `packages` (engine `test/cli/github*.test.ts` and `test/tool/registry*.test.ts`; core `test/review/*.test.ts` and `test/child-environment.test.ts`), `node-bundle` (build and Node smoke).
- **Lane policy tests** (`describe("@vector CI lane")`): triggers, the exact push trigger, the shared-group guard, the pull-request paths (`api/**`, `bun.lock`, `package.json` and the contract's others), read-only permissions, no reference to the `secrets` context, `github.token`, an index into `github` or `toJSON(github)` in any key or value of the parsed workflow, ignoring case (deviation 6), credential-free checkouts, pinned actions; the Valkey digest; every check the contract names is still present; every test step starts with `GITHUB_ACTIONS=false`, names files, and each named pattern matches a file; the web step has `VECTOR_TEST_REDIS_URL` and the service publishes that port.

### V02. Mention parsing: word boundaries and the App handle

- **Commit:** `2d49a86a` (`feat(github): V02 parse mentions with word boundaries and the App handle`), on top of `3d9e2f6d`, pushed to `origin/vector-at-mention`. Origin accepted the push; the pre-push hook's output was not kept, so no typecheck result is recorded for it. This package's progress step also filled in the V01 entry's hashes and lane run above.
- **Files:**
  - `packages/core/src/review/command.ts`: `parseReviewCommand` applies section 2.2, and `DEFAULT_MENTIONS` is a literal copy of `GithubAgents.mentions()`. It is still import-free and self-contained.
  - `packages/engine/src/cli/cmd/github.route.ts`: `TASK_MENTIONS = GithubAgents.mentions()`.
  - `packages/core/test/review/mention-cases.ts`: new, the shared table, not itself a test file (deviation 6).
  - `packages/core/test/review/command.test.ts`, `packages/engine/test/cli/github.route.test.ts` and `packages/engine/test/cli/github.route-script.test.ts`.
  - `packages/engine/test/cli/github.workflow.test.ts`: the two golden route conditions (deviation 1).
  - The deviations file (V02 entry).

  No shared file is touched, and `packages/engine/src/cli/vector-account.ts` is untouched. `command.ts` and `github.route.ts` are ours as the GitHub integration's owner. The lane needs no change: its core step runs `test/review/*.test.ts`, its engine step runs `test/cli/github*.test.ts`, and its pull-request paths cover `packages/core/test/review/**`.

- **Behaviour:**
  - A mention counts only as a word of its own (section 2.2), ignoring case.
  - Inline code spans are ignored, along with HTML comments, fences and quoted lines; a mention glued to a span does not count. Spans pair from the start of every line of a paragraph, backslash escapes follow CommonMark, only a line of spaces and tabs is blank, and a quote's code pairs across the lines that continue it (deviation 2). List markers can precede a fence or a `>`, and such a fence also ends with its list item (deviation 2). HTML comments are masked rather than cut, a comment that starts a line is an HTML block, and one elsewhere is read with the code spans (deviation 7).
  - The first line that starts with a mention gives the verb, as before.
  - `@<slug>` works through the mentions list; without the slug, a literal `@vector` is no mention (O1).
  - Trailing invisible characters do not keep a mention from ending its line.
  - Every pass is linear in the body's length.
  - Intended refusals that writers may notice: `/vectorx review` and other glued suffixes, mentions glued to code, `**/vector fix**`, `/vector... fix`, a mention after an unmatched backtick, and a mention after a span that wraps onto its line when another span follows it (deviation 2's cost). Quotes count as boundaries, as 2.2 lists them, so `'/vs fix'` and `/vector's` are tasks. Since the second review, also: a mention after an unclosed `<!--` that starts a line (GitHub hides the rest), a mention on a `- >` or `1. >` line, and one where a `<!--` that may be literal text pairs its backticks with later ones (deviation 7).
- **Tests:**
  - The shared table has 138 rows: the 45 bodies of the earlier `command.test.ts` corpus (`/vectorx review` now `none`, deviation 5) and 93 new ones. The new rows cover:
    - every refusal and acceptance case of 2.2, plus boundary extras;
    - inline code, including glued spans, multi-line spans, an unmatched backtick, a paragraph break and an escaped backtick;
    - the review's cases (17 rows): a list item and a heading after a stray backtick, nested pairings, the wrapped-span cost, three escape cases, blank lines of spaces and tabs against lines of a no-break or zero-width space, three quotes that continue across lines, and four bodies pinned as tasks that GitHub shows only as code (the constructs deviation 2 does not model);
    - the second review's cases (22 rows): eight list-item rows (fences after `- `, `1. ` and `* `, a fence that ends with its item at a line that opens a new one, a tab-indented line, and quotes after list markers), twelve HTML-comment rows (an HTML block across a blank line, one holding backticks, an unclosed one, the review's four bodies where a span holds a `<!--`, a comment line inside a span, a comment that takes a backtick, a trailing one, one that a quote's lazy line closes, and both readings of a `<!--`), and two fence bodies pinned as tasks that deviation 2 does not model;
    - quotes and fences, HTML comments across lines, and trailing invisible characters;
    - 17 rows with a slug: the verbs after `@vector-fixture`, refused look-alikes, and O1 with slug `vector` (`@vector review` reviews) and without it (`@vector review` is `none`).
  - `command.test.ts` (8 tests):
    - the table with `GithubAgents.mentions(slug)`;
    - `DEFAULT_MENTIONS` equals `GithubAgents.mentions()`;
    - no handle counts without the slug;
    - custom mentions, now with boundaries (`(/v+x) fix` is a task, `/v+xy fix` is not);
    - the `new Function` copy over the whole table, with no `import`, no `${{` and only printable ASCII in its `toString()`;
    - the module imports nothing (`scanImports` is `[]`, and the comment-free code has no `import`, `require(` or `from "…"`);
    - the source is printable ASCII, with a Windows checkout's CRLF line endings read as LF;
    - 23 hostile bodies at GitHub's 65,536-character limit stay under 250 ms each, for both the module and the embedded copy. The second review added five: inline comments holding backticks, many `<!--` sharing one `-->`, HTML blocks, list fences that their items end, and 65,533 spaces before a list marker.
  - `github.route-script.test.ts` (2 new tests, 10 in all): every table row runs through the route script the generated v2 workflow embeds. The row is built with the default mentions, or with `mentions: GithubAgents.mentions(slug)` baked in. Each row agrees with core's parser and gets the expected outputs and API calls: a refused mention, like a dismissal, makes no API call. The route job's `contains()` filter lets through every body the script reads a mention in, ignoring case as Actions does, and names `@vector-fixture` when the slug is baked in. The script is built for each call, keyed by both the App opt-in and the mentions, so no cache can mix them.
  - `github.route.test.ts` (2 new tests, 11 in all):
    - boundary and inline-code refusals and acceptances through `routeGithubEvent`;
    - `mentionsFrom("@Vector-Fixture,/vector,/vs")` lowercases the handle and routes `review`, `review full`, `pause` and a task after it, but not `@vector-fixture-bot` or `@vector`;
    - `TASK_MENTIONS` and `mentionsFrom(undefined)` equal `GithubAgents.mentions()`.
- **Review fixes (2026-10-30, before the commit):**
  - The inline-code pass could count a mention that GitHub shows only inside a span. This happened after a stray backtick in an earlier list item or heading, after an escaped backtick, and across a line of a no-break or zero-width space. The pass now pairs from every line's start, follows CommonMark's escapes, and ends a paragraph only at a line of spaces and tabs. It also keeps a quote's code pairing across the lines that continue the quote. Deviation 2 is rewritten to match, and names what is still not modelled.
  - "The source is printable ASCII" read `command.ts` from disk and failed on the CRLF line endings of a Windows checkout. The `unit (windows)` job of `test.yml` runs core's suite. The test now reads CRLF as LF. The `toString()` check stays LF-only, because Bun prints the function with LF, which a CRLF copy confirmed.
- **Second review (2026-10-30, before the commit):** four findings in two pairs, both real.
  - HTML comments were cut from the body before anything else. On GitHub a code span that opens before a `<!--` holds it as code, so cutting the comment could delete the span's closing backtick, and a line holding only a comment became blank and ended a paragraph that GitHub continues. Either way a mention that GitHub shows only as code counted (``Type `<!--` then --> and ` /vector fix ` ``), which reproduced against micromark. Fixed as deviation 7: comments are masked, not cut; a comment that starts a line is an HTML block; and one elsewhere is read with the code spans, in both readings of its `<!--`.
  - A fence or a quote after a list marker (`- ```sh`, ` 1. ``` `, `- > x`) was not detected, so `review`, `pause` or a task inside it counted; this predates V02. Fixed in deviation 2: list markers are skipped before fence and quote detection, and such a fence also ends with its list item. The fence cases that stay inexact are listed there, and two are pinned as tasks.
  - Deviation 2's "never counts a mention that GitHub shows only inside an inline span" is now qualified by its not-modelled list.
- **Local evidence (2026-10-30, Bun 1.3.14, each through `vector-serial`, one file at a time, `GITHUB_ACTIONS=false`, fresh XDG roots and `VECTOR_TEST_HOME`; each engine and core file was checked first for subprocesses, and none starts one):**
  - `packages/core`, after the second review's fixes:
    - `test/review/command.test.ts`: 8 pass, 0 fail (341 `expect()` calls);
    - `test/review/state.test.ts`, whose dismissal reading uses the parser: 42 pass, 0 fail (129);
    - `test/github-agents.test.ts`, before the review fixes, which do not touch it: 23 pass, 0 fail (132).
  - `packages/engine`, after the second review's fixes:
    - `test/cli/github.route.test.ts`: 11 pass, 0 fail (69);
    - `test/cli/github.route-script.test.ts`: 10 pass, 0 fail (429);
    - `test/cli/github.workflow.test.ts`: 24 pass, 0 fail (148).
  - Breakage checks before the review fixes, against `command.test.ts` and `github.route-script.test.ts`, each on the parser as it then stood and restored afterwards (the file compared byte for byte):
    - `startsWith` instead of the leading boundary;
    - no boundary before a mention;
    - no boundary after one;
    - no code-span stripping;
    - a span replaced by a space instead of `#`;
    - dropped quote lines not ending a paragraph;
    - no trailing-invisible trim.

    Each of these failed the table test in both files: the core table and its embedded-copy run, plus the custom-mentions test for the two boundary breakages. `DEFAULT_MENTIONS` in the old order failed core's equality test and the route-script `contains()` test. A type-only `import` failed "the module imports nothing". The earlier lazy comment regex, and the earlier end-anchored verb trim, each failed only the hostile-body test. A literal zero-width space in a character class failed only "the source is printable ASCII". The review fixes replaced the quote rule of the sixth check (see below).

  - Breakage checks after the review fixes, each on a scratch copy of the final parser, run against the shared table with Node 22 (`--experimental-strip-types`), not through `bun test`. Each failed at least one table row:
    - pairing from the paragraph's start only (3 rows);
    - no backslash escapes (2);
    - escapes applied to closing runs too (1);
    - a line blank once trimmed by JavaScript (2);
    - dropped quote lines ending the paragraph, as before the fixes (3);
    - no paragraph end where a quote starts (1);
    - overlapping spans not merged (1);
    - a quote counted open only after a line that starts with `>` (1), or only on the line after one (2).
  - Breakage checks after the second review, the same way. Each failed at least one table row:
    - comments cut before anything else, as before (6 rows);
    - no list markers before fence and quote detection (8);
    - a list item's fence not ending with the item (1);
    - a tab counted as one column there (1);
    - only the comment reading of a `<!--` (1), or no comment reading at all (4);
    - comments kept instead of removed (3);
    - an unclosed HTML block not running to the end (1), or no HTML-block rule (1);
    - an HTML block allowed at any indentation (1);
    - quoted lines masking their comment markers (1).
  - Differential check, from a scratch script under Node 22, against micromark 4.0.2 with micromark-extension-gfm 3.0.0 (a CommonMark and GFM parser already in `node_modules`; nothing installed). Random bodies are built from backticks, backslashes, list, heading, quote and setext markers, and no-break and zero-width spaces, each with one `/vector fix`. Fences, four-space indents, tabs and the constructs deviation 2 does not model (`<`, `[`, `|`, `www`) are left out. Across 12 seeds of 120,000 bodies, about 835,000 of which the parser accepted, it accepted none that micromark renders only inside code. While the fixes were made, the check found two counterexamples: a quote continued after a lazy line, and a quote after a list marker. Both are now table rows. On a smaller token set (seed 7, 60,000 bodies), the parser before the fixes accepted 152 such bodies.
  - Second-round differential check, the same way against the same micromark, comparing the parser before and after the second review's fixes. The verdict is whether micromark shows the mention outside code and outside the HTML comments a browser would read. With the first round's token set, 4 seeds of 60,000 bodies (about 121,800 accepted) give no body accepted that micromark renders only inside code. Adding comment tokens (`<!--`, `-->`, `<!-->` and comment lines), 2 seeds of 30,000 give 7 and 11 such bodies before the fixes and 5 and 10 after. Every one after the fixes is a comment that an HTML block inside a list item or quote leaves open, which the page's HTML closes later (deviation 7's not-modelled case). Adding list-marker and indented fences, 2 seeds of 30,000 give 203 and 200 before and 48 and 52 after. Those after are fences opened on a line of their own inside a list item that the item's end closes (deviation 2). Almost all of the new refusals are mentions that micromark shows only in a quote or a comment.
  - Timing with Bun 1.3.14, from a scratch script: the 18 hostile bodies parse in at most about 41 ms each. After the second review, the 23 of the test and six more parse in at most about 47 ms. The earlier comment regex took about 490 ms on the unclosed-`<!--` body. The earlier end-anchored trims took about 7 to 8.5 s on 65,536 characters of trailing dots or zero-width spaces.
  - The generated v2 workflows (default, App opt-in, and slug `vector-fixture`) are pure ASCII. Their route scripts contain no `${{`; the builder throws if one does.
  - Typechecks: `packages/core` and `packages/engine` passed, again after the review fixes and after the second review's. No `api/` file changed, and none imports `command.ts` yet, so the hosted API typecheck was not run.
  - Prettier `--check` is clean on the code, the tests, the fixture, the deviations file and this file.
  - Final gate, on the files as committed (fresh XDG roots and `VECTOR_TEST_HOME`, `GITHUB_ACTIONS=false`):

    - `packages/engine`: `test/cli/github.route-script.test.ts` 10 pass, 0 fail (429); `test/cli/github.route.test.ts` 11 pass, 0 fail (69); `test/cli/github.workflow.test.ts` 24 pass, 0 fail (148);
    - `packages/core` and `packages/engine` `bun typecheck` passed, and Prettier `--check` is clean on all nine changed files;

Also left to the lane: the rest of the engine GitHub and registry tests, the other core review tests, the hosted API typecheck and tests, the web GitHub tests, and the Node-bundle build and smoke.

The Node-bundle smoke (`script/smoke-node-bundle.ts`) only builds and loads the sidecar bundle; it never calls `buildRouteScript` or `parseReviewCommand`. Printers do rewrite the embedded text: Bun's own `toString()` here turned some string concatenations into template literals. No local or lane test runs the table through a script from either bundle. V30 or the owner's live steps cover it.

- **For later packages:**
  - V09: import `parseReviewCommand` from `../../packages/core/src/review/command.js` with `GithubAgents.mentions(slug)`. "The module imports nothing" and "the source is printable ASCII" guard that file. It uses `matchAll`, `Map` and regex lookaheads, and no lookbehind, `RegExp.escape` or `URL.parse`.
  - V14:
    - The v3 route script embeds the same function, and the workflow's `handle` gives `GithubAgents.mentions(slug)` for the script, the `contains()` condition and `MENTIONS`.
    - The v2 builder's "byte-identical" baseline is its output after V02 (deviation 1).

  - V18: `github.handler.ts` (about lines 835–859) still finds the mention for the prompt with substring `includes` and an exact-body match, and `isFixCommand` (about line 1850) still uses `startsWith` and its own fence toggle. Both run only after routing accepted a valid mention, but they can disagree with it. For example, with `/vectorize this` on the first line and `/vector fix` on the second, the route is a task, yet `isFixCommand` reads the first line and says no. V18 should strip and read the mention with the same boundaries.
  - No package yet: the Markdown the parser still reads differently from GitHub. Fences: a backtick in the info string, four-space indents, an ordered marker other than `1.` that continues a paragraph, and a fence on a line of its own inside a list item, which the item's end closes. Indented code blocks: section 2.2 does not name them, so `    /vector review` after a blank line counts although GitHub shows it as code. And comments that GitHub's HTML leaves open (deviation 7). Exact handling needs a model of list items and quotes as containers. Deviations 2 and 7 record them.
  - V23 and V24: feed `GithubAgents.slugFromInstallUrl(installUrl)` into the builder's `mentions`. Workflows people already installed keep their old embedded parser until they are reinstalled. A newer CLI that re-routes in `vector github run` can only refuse more than the route job did, never accept more, because the route job gates first.

### V03. Actions safety floor: withheld tools, env stripping, repo-work policy

- **Commit:** `02a221e8` (`feat(github): V03 add the Actions safety floor`), on top of `2d49a86a`, pushed to `origin/vector-at-mention`. No pre-push hook result is recorded for it. The phase review's fixes follow in their own commit (below).
- **Files:**
  - `packages/engine/src/cli/cmd/github.policy.ts` (new, import-free): `REPO_WORK_POLICY` and `UNTRUSTED_DATA_RULE` (the 2.17 text), `githubSystemInstructions(repository)` and `untrustedJson(value)` (deviation 7).
  - `packages/engine/src/cli/cmd/github.handler.ts` (ours): `githubRun` pins the policy before anything can reach a model and never unpins it, and deletes `ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL` and `_TOKEN` from `process.env` once the App exchange settles (deviations 1 and 8). Nothing else in `githubRun` changes.
  - Shared files in contract 0.2: `packages/core/src/child-environment.ts` (the INV-S5 filter, deviation 5) and `packages/engine/src/tool/registry.ts` (`actionsWithheld(id)` and the `all()` filter, deviation 4; `actionsWithheldServer(name)`, deviation 2, replaced in the phase review by `actionsWithheldServers` and `actionsWithheldMcpTool`).
  - Shared files outside contract 0.2, each one small: `packages/engine/src/session/tools.ts` (the guard in the MCP loop and in the three MCP resource tools, deviation 2), `packages/engine/src/session/system.ts` (`SystemPrompt.mcp` leaves out a withheld server's instructions, deviation 2), `packages/engine/src/tool/research-browser.ts` (`available()` is false on Actions, deviation 3) and `packages/engine/src/session/llm/request.ts` (`LLMRequestPrep.pin`, deviation 1).
  - Tests: `packages/core/test/child-environment.test.ts` (six tests in `describe("on GitHub Actions")`, and one real-child test after it), `packages/engine/test/tool/registry-actions.test.ts` (new) and `packages/engine/test/cli/github.policy.test.ts` (new).
  - The deviations file (V03 entry) and this file (the V02 entry's hash and lane run, and this entry).

  `packages/engine/src/cli/vector-account.ts` is untouched. Nothing is generated: no public Protocol, HttpApi or SDK surface changed. The lane needs no change: its engine step runs `test/cli/github*.test.ts` and `test/tool/registry*.test.ts`, its core step runs `test/child-environment.test.ts`, and its pull-request paths and the lane policy's list of named checks already cover all three.

- **Behaviour:**
  - With `GITHUB_ACTIONS=true`, whatever bridge variables are set:
    - the registry never offers `browser`, `vector_cloud`, `research_browser` or any id matching `/^computer(?:$|[_-])/i`, a plugin's included;
    - a `computer` MCP server (since the phase review, any server whose own name or one of whose tools' own names is computer use) is withheld whole: its tools are not offered, `list_mcp_resources` and `list_mcp_resource_templates` leave it out, `read_mcp_resource` answers that it is not connected, and its instructions are left out of the system prompt;
    - `research_search` and `research_read`'s rendering send nothing to the desktop's research browser.
  - The check is read on every call, so a registry built before the variable was set is filtered too.
  - On Actions, every child that `untrustedChildEnvironment` builds loses:
    - `ACTIONS_*`;
    - `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GH_TOKEN`, `VECTOR_CLI_TOKEN`, `VECTOR_BIND_CODE` and `VECTOR_TICKET`;
    - every name ending `_TOKEN`, `_KEY`, `_SECRET` or `_PASSWORD`, compared upper-cased;
    - every name `VECTOR_SECRET_ENV_NAMES` lists, from any source.

    Such children include the shell tool, the pty, LSP servers, formatters, MCP servers, plugin installs and infrastructure git, whose credentials travel in `GIT_CONFIG_*`, not in a secret-named variable. Outside Actions nothing changes.

  - Every model request a `vector github run` sends carries `REPO_WORK_POLICY` for its repository and `UNTRUSTED_DATA_RULE` in its system instructions, after the session's own text. That covers subagents, the judge, follow-ups, compaction and the quick lane.

- **Tests:**
  - `child-environment.test.ts`:
    - "Actions runs hide runner, GitHub, provider and named secrets from children" (INV-S5's name: `ACTIONS_*`, the five named credentials, `AWS_SESSION_TOKEN`, `HF_TOKEN`, `DEEPSEEK_KEY`, `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, `DB_PASSWORD`, `SIGNING_SECRET` and a listed `MY_PROVIDER_CREDS` hidden; `PATH`, `CI`, `GITHUB_REPOSITORY`, `GITHUB_WORKSPACE`, `RUNNER_TEMP` kept);
    - "outside Actions, GITHUB_TOKEN is kept" (unset and `false`);
    - lower-case Windows spellings;
    - a later source cannot lift the filter or empty the list;
    - the agent marker and the Vector Local floor still apply after the filter;
    - "with no source, the process's own environment is filtered": the no-argument call most callers make (infrastructure git, LSP servers, core's shell helper), with `GITHUB_ACTIONS=true`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, two `ACTIONS_*` values and a `_KEY` name planted in `process.env` and put back afterwards;
    - after the describe, "a real child of a process on an Actions runner reads none of its credentials": the same, read back from a real Bun child. Its name stays outside the local `-t "on GitHub Actions"` filter, so it runs only in the lane.

    - its control outside Actions;
    - one registry instance built outside Actions, then on Actions, then outside again (critique item 4);

Its control offers all of them, and lists and reads the screen;

    - its control: outside Actions, `research_search` sends `research.search` to the bridge.

The pre-push hook's output was not kept, so no result is recorded for it. Its lane run is under "CI (seen)" at the end of this block.**

It is replaced by `ToolRegistry.actionsWithheldServers(servers, keys)` and `actionsWithheldMcpTool(key, withheld)`, which ask core's new `GithubAgents.computerUse(name)` (section 2.1) of the server's name and its tools' own names (V03 deviation 2).

- **For later packages:**
  - V16: the child filter and the in-process deletion of the OIDC pair are defence in depth only. `/proc/<pid>/environ` keeps the engine's launch environment, the pair and provider keys included, for any same-user process in a v2 single-job workflow.
  - V17: keep `LLMRequestPrep.pin(...)` before the auth exchange, and add no unpin to `githubRun`: background subagents, the turns their notes start and the titles `effectCmd` waits for still send requests after `githubRun` returns, until the instance is disposed. A test that runs the lifted helpers in-process unpins in its own scope, as `github.policy.test.ts`'s `pinned` does. That file reads the pin from `github.handler.ts`, so update its source check if the lift moves it.
  - V18:
    - the agent phase pins `githubSystemInstructions` before any session and never unpins it, as `githubRun` does;
    - the lifted prompt builders write every body with `untrustedJson`.
    - Subagents need nothing more: the pin covers every session of the process.
    - `githubRun` does not wait for background subagents before it commits, posts and returns, as `vector run` does (`cli/cmd/run.ts`); disposing the instance stops them. The agent phase decides whether to wait.
  - V20: the review job (`github.review.ts`) does not pin the policy yet.
  - V14 and V23: write `VECTOR_SECRET_ENV_NAMES` for every key line. Until then, on v2 a provider key whose name lacks the suffix stays visible to children (deviation 5).
  - V29's docs say that on Actions:
    - children see no `ACTIONS_*` value and no name ending `_TOKEN`, `_KEY`, `_SECRET` or `_PASSWORD`: `NPM_TOKEN` and `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN` for installs, an MCP server's token variables (even when its `environment` sets them; the run's log names those once), a step's own secrets that a command would inherit, and `gh` in the model's shell included;
    - the research tools need the desktop app.

    They do not claim more than the suffix rule for v2 workflows.

  - Hands E5: the `computer` registration and its availability check must call `GithubAgents.onActionsRunner` and `GithubAgents.withheldOnActions`. The registry's `all()` filter already withholds a `computer` id, which `registry-actions.test.ts` proves with a plugin stand-in. E5 also guards core's V2 registry, which V03 does not touch (deviation 4).
  - `test.yml`'s app e2e job is unaffected: it starts only the app's Vite dev server (`playwright.config.ts`'s `webServer`) and answers the engine's calls with `mockVectorServer` and `page.route`. The engine runs that do have `GITHUB_ACTIONS=true` are `test.yml`'s HttpApi exerciser step (`bun run test:httpapi` in `packages/engine`, in process) and the Node-bundle smoke in `test.yml` and the lane. Neither depends on a withheld tool or a stripped name: the exerciser's tool routes only check that a list comes back, and its shell and pty children run `printf` and `sleep`; the smoke calls the Read and memory tools.

### V03c. Lane green: the review step's end-to-end tests after the Actions child filter

- **Commit:** `19f986ac` (`fix(github): V03c start the review CLI the way the runner does in end-to-end tests`), on top of `19cb3d5a`, pushed to `origin/vector-at-mention` (still at `19f986ac` when V04 started). The pre-push hook's output was not kept, so no typecheck result is recorded for it.
- **Files:** `packages/engine/test/lib/cli-process.ts` (a shared engine test harness outside contract 0.2: `VectorCli.actionsStep` and its implementation, and a note on `spawn`); `packages/engine/test/cli/github.review.test.ts` (inside `describe("vector github review: the workflow's command end to end")` only: its comment, the four tests switched to `actionsStep`, and three new tests); the deviations file (V03c entry) and this file (the V03 entry's pointer and the phase review's commit and lane run, and this entry). No product source changed: `packages/core/src/child-environment.ts` and `cross-spawn-spawner.ts` are untouched, and so is `packages/engine/src/cli/vector-account.ts`. Nothing is generated.
- **Cause and decision** (deviation 1): the four tests started the review step as a child of Vector, through core's spawner, so V03's filter withheld the `GITHUB_TOKEN` (and in the fourth test the `DEEPINFRA_API_KEY`) they configured. No product path starts a Vector CLI through Vector's spawner on a runner with credentials in that child's environment, so the product is right and the filter gets no pass-through. The harness gains `vector.actionsStep`, which starts the CLI as GitHub starts a step.
- **Tests** (`github.review.test.ts`, end-to-end block, now seven tests):
  - the four tests, unchanged but for `vector.actionsStep` in place of `vector.spawn`;
  - `no credential-named variable reaches the step's children: the review's git fetches see no GITHUB_TOKEN, ACTIONS_* or listed secret` (deviation 2): the step's CLI reviews, and an upload-pack recorder in the checkout's git config shows that its git fetches got `GITHUB_ACTIONS`, `GITHUB_REPOSITORY`, `VECTOR_REVIEW_PR` and `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0`, and none of `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `ACTIONS_RUNTIME_TOKEN`, `ACTIONS_RESULTS_URL`, `NPM_TOKEN` and a listed `MY_PROVIDER_CREDS`. It checks names only: `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0` is git's auth header for Vector's own fetches and holds the step's token, by design, and the test pins it;
  - "started as a child through Vector's own spawner, the same step gets no GITHUB_TOKEN" (deviation 2): exit 1, `GITHUB_TOKEN is not set`, no request to the fake GitHub, no model call;
  - `a step stopped mid-fetch leaves nothing running: the CLI's own finalizers stop the git its group kill cannot reach` (deviation 2): origin's upload-pack never answers and writes a beat every 0.2 s; once it runs, the test interrupts the step, and no beat follows the release.
- **Local evidence (2026-10-30, Bun 1.3.14, each through `vector-serial`, from `packages/engine`).** Every test run used `GITHUB_ACTIONS=false CI=true VECTOR_EXPERIMENTAL_DISABLE_FILEWATCHER=false`, `TMPDIR`, `XDG_*` and `VECTOR_TEST_HOME` under `/tmp/vector-V03c`, and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `DEEPINFRA_API_KEY`, `GITHUB_TOKEN` and `GH_TOKEN` unset, so `actionsStep`, which passes the test process's environment on, could hand no real credential to the CLI. The coordinator allowed `github.review.test.ts`, which starts Bun CLI processes, to run alone this way.
  - Before any edit, on `19cb3d5a`: `bun test --timeout 30000 test/cli/github.review.test.ts -t "the workflow's command end to end"`: 0 pass, 4 fail, 36 filtered out (2 `expect()` calls). The run printed "Withheld from one: GITHUB_TOKEN." and "Withheld from one: DEEPINFRA_API_KEY.", and each child's stderr held `GITHUB_TOKEN is not set. Provide the repository Actions token.`: the lane's failure, reproduced.
  - After the change, the whole file once: 42 pass, 0 fail (259 `expect()` calls), in 61 s. After the review fixes below, the whole file again: 43 pass, 0 fail (261 `expect()` calls), in 55 s, and no upload-pack, `git fetch` or CLI process was left running afterwards. It now holds 43 tests, 40 before.
  - Breakage checks, each on a backup under `/tmp/vector-V03c/backup`, restored and compared byte for byte afterwards. Each failed exactly the one test named:
    - m1, the first end-to-end test put back on `vector.spawn`, run with `-t "an opened pull request is reviewed"`: that test, "expected exit 0, got 1" with `GITHUB_TOKEN is not set`;
    - m2a, `child-environment.ts` computing the filter from the configured sources only (`actionsSecrets(list.slice(1))`), run with `-t "the step's children get none of its credentials"` and, after the review's rename, again with `-t "no credential-named variable reaches"`: the recorder test, whose git fetches then saw all five planted credentials;
    - m2b, `child-environment.ts` letting through every name a later source configures, run with `-t "same step gets no GITHUB_TOKEN"`: the control test, whose CLI then reviewed and exited 0 after about 5 s.
    - m3, the first version's release (SIGKILL to the CLI's group at once), run with `-t "a step stopped mid-fetch"` before the review fix was applied: the release test, whose beat file grew from 2 to 12 bytes in the second after the release. `ps` then showed the review's `git fetch` with its own session id (= its pid) and parent pid 1, and its upload-pack under it, still running; they were stopped by hand with SIGTERM to that group. With the fixed release the test passes and nothing is left running.
  - The first version's scratch check of the release (a detached `sh` whose background `sleep` holds stdout) showed only that the group kill stops a process in the CLI's own group. It did not cover the CLI's spawner children, which run in sessions of their own (deviation 1, "How `actionsStep` stops the CLI").
  - Typecheck: `packages/engine` `bun typecheck` (its tests included) passed, exit 0, before and again after the review fixes.
  - Final gate before the commit, after the last edit to any test or harness file (`github.review.test.ts`, 23:55 UTC): `-t "the workflow's command end to end"`: 7 pass, 0 fail, 36 filtered out (24 `expect()` calls), in 32 s; `packages/engine` `bun typecheck` again: passed, exit 0. `git status` then held only the four files this entry names; the m2a backup of `child-environment.ts` was restored, and `packages/core` matches `19cb3d5a`.
  - Prettier `--check`: clean on `cli-process.ts`, on both docs and on every line this package changed in `github.review.test.ts`; that file's one report (about line 977) was already there on `19cb3d5a`, and it is left as it is.
  - Not run locally, left to the lane: `github.run-routing.test.ts` (it starts the CLI through the unchanged `vector.spawn`; the exception covered only `github.review.test.ts`), the rest of the lane's engine step, and the core, hosted API and Node-bundle jobs, none of which this package touches. `test.yml`'s Windows engine job, which runs `github.review.test.ts` once this branch reaches a pull request or `main`, is the first to run `actionsStep`'s Windows path and the recorder under Git for Windows' shell.
- **Review fixes (2026-10-30), three findings, all fixed:**
  1. and 3. `actionsStep`'s comment and deviation 1 said its group kill also stopped git and the CLI's other children. It does not: core's spawner starts each child in a session of its own on POSIX, and the release sent SIGKILL at once, so the CLI's finalizers, which stop them, never ran. The release now sends SIGTERM to the CLI's group, waits up to 5 s for the CLI to exit, then sends SIGKILL; the comment and deviation 1 say what each step reaches. The new release test (m3) fails on the old release and passes on the new one. The two pipe reads after the release get no time limit of their own; deviation 1 says why.
  2. The recorder test's name, its comment and deviation 2 said git's children got none of the step's credentials, while git's fetch environment holds the token as the auth header in `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0`. The claim is narrowed to credential-named variables, and the recorder now also probes `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0` and the test asserts that it arrives, so the exception is pinned. Its comment no longer says MCP and language servers start through core's spawner: they meet the same filter on their own paths.
- **CI (seen, recorded by V04):** lane run 41147776493 (`Vector at-mention`, push on `19f986ac`, 2026-10-31 00:00–00:02 UTC): success in all three jobs, the lane's first green run since V03 (runs 41134319880 and 41139759209 failed; deviation V03c 1).
  - `engine and core` (job 124376803239): every step succeeded, "Run engine GitHub tests" and "Run core GitHub tests" included. The end of the job log shows the core step's summary: 384 pass, 0 fail, 1800 `expect()` calls across 21 files. The engine step's summary lies earlier in the log and was not read here.
  - `hosted API` (job 124376803541) and `desktop engine bundle (node)` (job 124376803923): every step succeeded; their logs were not read.
- **For later packages:** deviation 3. A workflow step's end-to-end test that needs the runner's credentials starts the CLI with `actionsStep`; a test of the CLI as Vector's child keeps `spawn`.

Codium's `api/_lib/model-plan-account.ts` and `model-plan-config.ts` are imported only. `packages/engine/src/cli/vector-account.ts` is untouched. Nothing is generated.
- **The module** (contract 2.3, exact shapes): `CodiumPlanId`, `RUN_SOURCES` (Codium's order), `RunSource`, `ENABLED_SOURCES` (built as Codium builds it), `RunTrigger` (readonly, copied from `origin/codium`), `CloudAgentLimits`, `CloudAgentAllowance`; `cloudAgentEntitlement(accountId, input, fetcher = fetch)`, whose body is the one line A59 replaces; `cloudAgentRevokedBefore(accountId, fetcher = fetch)`, which resolves `undefined`. Below a marked line sits the local backing A59 deletes: the deep-frozen `CLOUD_AGENT_LIMITS`, `createCloudAgentEntitlement`, and the default instance. In order, it answers `service-off` unless `modelPlansEnabled()` and `VECTOR_CLOUD_AGENTS_ENABLED === "true"` (both read on every call), then `source-unavailable` outside `ENABLED_SOURCES`, then reads the plan through the 60-s per-account cache (deviation 2). The default `plan()` reads `readModelPlanAccount` for `closing`, then `modelPlanStatus(...).active` (deviation 3). An active answer maps to the table's limits (a fresh copy), `allowanceSeconds = hours × 3600`, `meterEpoch = "p" + periodStart` in ms, `periodEnd` in ms (deviation 5) and `premiumModels` on Max and Ultra, with no `revokedBefore`; a plan id the table does not hold gives `none` (deviation 8).
- **Tests** (`cloud-agents-entitlement.test.ts`, 18 tests; no subprocess, no Redis, no network: every fetcher is injected, the fake store is `https://kv.fixture.invalid`, and Stripe and OpenRouter are answered by the same injected fetcher):
  - local backing: each plan's values (literals for every `CodiumPlan.PLANS` id, the exact key sets, no `revokedBefore`); INV-K3 through results only (caps never fall Plus → Ultra, `atOnce ≥ 1`, scheduled 0 on Plus and Pro, `runCeilingShare` in (0, 1], no key matching `/usd|price|credit|cost|\$/i`); INV-P2 (a JSON scan of every plan's result and every local refusal); service-off from all 15 switch combinations other than both `"true"` (unset, `"1"`, `"TRUE"`, `"true"`) with no request, then `none` for an unknown account with store GETs only; reserved sources (`slack`, `linear`, `jira`) and unknown ones (`github-review`, `__proto__`, `constructor`, `""`) give `source-unavailable` with no plan read, service-off comes first, every enabled source passes, and the default refuses a reserved source with no request; inactive, closing and five plan ids the table does not hold (`vector-1000`, `toString`, `__proto__`, `constructor`, `hasOwnProperty`); a closing account through the default after exactly one store GET and no billing read; two active Max subscriptions through the default (deviation 7), each with the epoch and period end in ms and no store write: one whose monthly key is not provisioned yet, with no OpenRouter request, and one holding the period's key, the usual paid state, where an OpenRouter failure rejects with 503 `MODEL_PLAN_UPSTREAM` and the next call reads the key again (one GET per call, no other OpenRouter request) and grants the same allowance;
  - the cache: one `plan()` call at T and T + 59 999 ms, a second at T + 60 000, one per account, a fresh read for a call dated before the cached one; parallel calls share one read; a rejected read rejects that call and is not kept; the switch and a reserved source win over a warm cache; a slow read that fails after a newer read replaced it leaves the newer one cached; each result's limits are the caller's own;
  - the seams A59 swaps: "revokedBefore seam" (INV-K6: `undefined` for three accounts, with and without a fetcher, no request; its type equals Codium's `runTokensRevokedBefore`); the entitlement types against 2.3 and §4.18 (deviation 9); the `CLOUD_AGENT_LIMITS` scan over `api/` and `packages/`, which must read more than 1000 files including known files from both trees and finds the name only in the module and this test.
- **Local evidence (2026-10-31, Bun 1.3.14, each through `vector-serial`, one file at a time, `GITHUB_ACTIONS=false`, `XDG_*`, `VECTOR_TEST_HOME` and `TMPDIR` under `/tmp/vector-V04`):**

The test file under 25 module mutations: no cache (4 cache tests fail); rejected reads kept ("a failed read is not kept"); the source check before the switch ("each reserved or unknown source…"); a warm cache skipping both checks ("the switch and the reserved sources win over a warm cache" only); Max `scheduledPerDay` 4 (the per-plan values, the default Max test and the caller's-copy test); premium models on Pro (the per-plan values); the epoch in seconds, and without the `p` (the per-plan values and the default Max test); the default `plan()` giving the start in seconds, or start and end swapped (the default Max test only); the default ignoring `VECTOR_CLOUD_AGENTS_ENABLED`, ignoring `MODEL_PLANS_ENABLED`, or reading the switch case-insensitively (the switch test); the closing read dropped (the closing test); `revokedBefore: 0` on active results (the per-plan values and the default Max test); a `priceUsd` field in limits (5 tests, INV-K3 and INV-P2 among them); the `hasOwn` guard dropped (the table-does-not-hold test); the cache dropping an entry without the identity check (the slow-read race test); limits not copied (the caller's-copy test); the boundary at `≤ 60 s` (the cache test, and the slow-read test by timeout); a call dated earlier reusing the cache (the cache test); `jira` enabled (the source test); hours × 60 (the per-plan values and the default Max test); `cloudAgentRevokedBefore` returning 0 ("revokedBefore seam"). ### V05. Repo bindings, bind codes and generations

- **Commits:** a docs commit, `docs(github): name the V05 binding fields and parameters in sections 2.5 and 2.7`, on top of `22f9c004`, which changes only the contract (2.5's signatures, `BindIdentity`, `RedeemInput`, `takeoverAllowed`, `recordRepoBinding`'s result, the O6, generation and suspension rules; 2.7's stored field names and the bind-code JSON; 2.14's `transferred` and `deleted` bullets, which now read the binding first; 2.18's bind-code row; and, from the second review, 2.7's and 2.18's `binder-left:<account>` rows, 2.5's binder-left paragraph and `suspendBinding`'s `account`, and the binder-left hiding rule in 2.8, 2.16 and the V11 and V26 lines; and, from the third review, 2.5's `suspendBinding` comment, suspension paragraph and binder-left paragraph for a `binder-left` that lands after the binding read is gone or replaced), as the section 2 preamble asks; then this package's commit, `feat(github): V05 add repo bindings, bind codes and generations`. Recorded by V06: the docs commit is `47f92e71` and the package commit `2ba59d90`, both pushed to `origin/vector-at-mention` (still at `2ba59d90` when V06 started). The pre-push hook's output was not kept, so no typecheck result is recorded for it.
- **Files:** `api/_lib/cloud-agents-binding.ts` (new); `packages/web/test/cloud-agents-binding.test.ts` (new); the deviations file (V05 entry); this file (V04's commit and lane run, and this entry). No shared file is touched, and `packages/engine/src/cli/vector-account.ts` is untouched. Nothing is generated. The lane is unchanged: its web step and pull-request paths already take `test/cloud-agents-*.test.ts` (V04 deviation 1), which now matches two files.
- **The module** (contract 2.5 and 2.7, deviations V05 1–13): `RepoBinding`, `SuspendReason`, `BindIdentity` and `RedeemInput`; the key builders `bindingKey` and `generationKey` (the `bindings:<account>`, `binder-left:<account>` and `bind-code:<sha256>` builders stay private until V11's erase and run list need them); `createBindCode`, `peekBindCode`, `takeoverAllowed`, `recordRepoBinding`, `redeemAndBind`, `bindingFor`, `bindingsForAccount`, `revokeBinding`, `touchBinding`, `suspendBinding` and `renameBinding`, each with a trailing `store = persistentStore`. It is Node-only code with no Bun API, and imports `GithubAgents` (for `BIND_CODE_SECONDS`) by relative path from the import-free `packages/core/src/github-agents.ts`. Every input is checked with Effect Schema field schemas before any store command (400 `INVALID_REQUEST`, or the one 403 `BIND_CODE_INVALID` for a malformed code); stored data is decoded strictly with the same schemas (503 on anything unknown, missing or malformed). Every write is one Lua script that checks everything before its first write and gets every key in `KEYS`: the redeem (code, binding, counter, the code account's set, the judged account's set, the code account's binder-left set), unbind, touch, suspend (binding, counter, the binding account's binder-left set) and rename. Every read runs inside a script too, so replies are flat arrays on RESP2 and RESP3 alike. `suspendBinding` takes the binding as the caller read it (`repositoryId`, `generation` and `account`) and leaves any later binding alone (deviation 12); `binder-left` also goes into the read account's binder-left set, which outlives the binding, also when it lands after that binding is gone or another account's later binding has replaced it, and only a later binding of the same account skips it (deviation 13).
- **Tests** (`cloud-agents-binding.test.ts`, 39 tests, no subprocess). The harness follows `github-app-api.test.ts`: a loopback `VECTOR_TEST_REDIS_URL` (without it, one failing test; deviation 10), Bun's `RedisClient` with `enableOfflineQueue: false`, and an in-process `Bun.serve` fake of the store's REST endpoint that checks the bearer, records every command, and can answer 503 (`down`), answer an unrecognised result (`corrupt`), or hold the first two requests until both have arrived (the gate, so parallel calls always interleave at their first store command). It saves and restores `KV_REST_API_URL`, `KV_REST_API_TOKEN` and the two `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_*` names, uses fresh UUID accounts and random 9-digit repository ids in every test, never flushes, and deletes every key it touched, generation counters included, in `afterAll`.
  - Bind codes: the format and `expiresAt = now + 600 000`; the one `SET … EX 600 NX` under `sha256(code)`, no command holding the code, the exact JSON key set with and without the optional fields, TTL in (590, 600]; 16 invalid inputs answer 400 with no store command; a peek returns `{account, defaultRef}` three times with three GETs, the key and its TTL left in place; seven malformed codes get the 403 from both peek and redeem with no store command; one table of eleven match cases (same, another letter case, `exp − 1`, no ids stored, exactly `exp`, after `exp`, another name, id or user, an unknown code, a used code) through both `peekBindCode` and `redeemAndBind` with the same outcome, a refused redeem leaving the code in place, and every refusal the same status, code and message.
  - `redeemAndBind`: exactly one EVAL consumes the code and writes exactly the 2.7 fields (`defaultRef = identity.ref`, visibility `private` when absent, `public` and `internal` when given); codes whose `defaultRef` names another branch than the token's ref bind the token's ref, returned, stored and read back; a code whose repository is spelled in another case than the token's binds the token's spelling, returned, stored and read back; 17 invalid inputs answer 400 with no store command and leave the code usable; a parallel double redeem through the gate gives one binding and one 403; two accounts binding one repository through the gate give one binding, one 409 `REPO_BOUND_ELSEWHERE`, the repository in the winner's set only, generation 1, and the loser's code still peekable; a binder that changed since the caller judged O6 (`current` absent, or a third account) gives `changed` and 409 before any write, while the binder's own account still rebinds; a valid code redeemed for an account other than its own (the repository unbound, or already bound to that account) gives `code` and the same 403, writes no binding, counter or set member, and leaves the code peekable for its own account.
  - Generations: bind 1, rebind 2 (a fresh binding: new `boundAt`, no `lastUsedAt`), unbind 3 with the counter's TTL −1, bind again 4, first suspension 5, takeover 6, touch and rename no change; the binding's `generation` equals `GET gen` after every step.
  - O6: eleven cases, each through `takeoverAllowed` and `redeemAndBind` with the same answer: refused at a last use 30 days minus 1 ms ago, allowed at exactly 30 days; never used, measured from `boundAt` the same way; refused for a fresh binding; allowed at once when suspended (each reason), when the owner id changed, when the allowance is inactive, or for a verified admin. A refusal leaves the binding, both sets and the taker's code as they were; a takeover writes the taker's fresh binding, bumps the generation once, and moves the repository between the sets. `takeoverAllowed` allows the binder's own account and an unbound repository.
  - `bindingsForAccount`: the account's own bindings only, newest `boundAt` first, ties by id, with ids rising against that order so no unsorted order passes; a member with no binding and a member bound to another account are skipped; exactly two EVALs, one for an empty set; unbind and takeover leave the list.
  - `revokeBinding`: false and no bump for another account, true and one bump for the binder, false again after.
  - `touchBinding`: only forward, no generation change, and never a recreated binding after unbind.
  - `suspendBinding` and `renameBinding`: the first suspension bumps once; a repeat or another reason keeps the first one; `binder-left` replaces `transferred` and then stays through `transferred` and `deleted` (both orders); a rename changes only the name; invalid names, reasons and suspension targets (repository id `0`; generation 0, −1, 1.5, NaN, 2^53 or a string) answer 400 with no command; a suspension with the binding read before a rebind, a takeover, an unbind, an unbind and a new bind, an unbind and another account's bind, a takeover that was then unbound or suspended, a suspension and then a rebind, or a rebind that was then suspended leaves the later binding (or none) and the counter exactly as they were, for every reason; `transferred` and `deleted` write no member in any of them, and `binder-left` adds the repository to the read account's set exactly when no later binding of that account holds it (the takeover and unbind cases), never to the taker's; the binding read before another caller's first suspension still takes `binder-left` over `transferred`; a generation the binding never had, an account it never had, a gone binding with a generation the counter never passed, and another account's binding bound no later than the one read (suspended or not) change nothing and write no member; `binder-left` (fresh, or replacing `transferred`) adds the repository to the binding account's binder-left set whatever the visibility, `transferred` alone adds nothing, the member survives a takeover by another account and an unbind, and only the same account's own later bind removes it (also when it takes the repository back from the taker, whose own member stays); suspend (both reasons), rename and touch on an unknown repository create no key, binder-left set included.
  - Fail closed (INV-S16): with the store down, and again with an unrecognised reply, all 20 calls (every function, for a bound and an unbound repository) reject with 503 `PERSISTENT_STORE_UNAVAILABLE`, and afterwards the binding, the counter, the binder-left set and the code are unchanged; twelve malformed binding hashes written directly (an unknown field, a missing generation or binder, generation 0 or a word, an unknown visibility or suspension, a non-UUID account, another repository id, an exponent owner id, a negative bind time, a tag ref) answer 503 from `bindingFor` and from `bindingsForAccount`, and a member that is not an id fails the list with 503; four corrupt bind codes answer 503 from peek and redeem with nothing written; a corrupt binding fails a takeover with 503, both with no grounds for one (no flags, a fresh binding) and with an admin after 30 days, and leaves the hash, the counter, the taker's set and the code: `boundAt` a word, in exponent form, no account, an empty or unknown suspension, an owner id in exponent form or past 2^53 − 1, a hex or space-padded last use; a corrupt counter fails the redeem with nothing written and the code still usable; an account set of another type fails a redeem and an unbind with nothing written; a binder-left set of another type fails a binder-left suspension and a rebind with nothing written; a corrupt counter fails a binder-left suspension with no suspension and no member written; an unknown or empty stored suspension fails a suspension with 503 and nothing written; a stored `generation` of `two`, `0`, `1.0` or empty fails a suspension with 503 and nothing written; a stored account that is not a lowercase UUID, or missing, fails a `binder-left` with 503 and nothing written; once the binding is gone, a counter of `many`, `0` or `02` fails a `binder-left` with 503 and no member, and so does a binder-left set of another type.
  - Types: `RepoBinding` equals 2.5's shape, and `GithubIdentity` is assignable to `BindIdentity` (checked by `packages/web`'s typecheck).
- **Local evidence (2026-10-31, Bun 1.3.14, each through `vector-serial`, one file at a time, `GITHUB_ACTIONS=false`, `XDG_*`, `VECTOR_TEST_HOME` and `TMPDIR` under `/tmp/vector-V05`):**

The account-set type check first survived too; the corrupt-data test now covers it.

- **Second review (2026-10-31, same conditions; Redis 7.0.15 on a new random loopback port under `/tmp/vector-V05/review2/redis`, stopped with `redis-cli shutdown nosave` afterwards).** Four findings, all real:
  - The binding's `defaultRef` being the token's ref, never the code's, was untested: no redeemed code carried a `defaultRef`. Confirmed: with the redeem storing `code.defaultRef or ARGV[9]`, all 37 tests passed. Fixed with the default-ref test, which now redeems codes naming another branch than the token's (deviation 9).
  - `binder-left` lived only on the binding hash, which a takeover (allowed at once under O6), a rebind or an unbind deletes, while deviation 6 told V11 to hide run detail by reading it there; after any of them nothing recorded that the binder lost access, so INV-P3 would fail for the account's 90-day run list. Confirmed by the new test against the old module (no record of it anywhere). Fixed as deviation 13: the `binder-left:<account>` set, written by the suspend script and cleared only by the same account's later bind (and V11's erase); `suspendBinding` takes the binding's `account` too.
  - The stored visibility is the bind token's and never updated, so a visibility-gated hiding would leak a repository made private later. Fixed in the same deviation: the hiding no longer reads visibility (2.8, 2.16, V11, V26; deviation 2), and the set is written whatever the visibility (the new test binds that repository as public).
  - The redeem script decided O6 on stored fields it did not validate: any `suspended` value (the empty string included) and a malformed `ownerId` were grounds for a takeover where every reader answers 503. Confirmed against the old module: an empty `suspended` on a fresh binding let another account take it over with no flags ("accepted"). Fixed: the redeem checks `ownerId`, `boundAt`, `lastUsedAt` and `suspended` with the reader's rules in Lua before O6, and the suspend script refuses an unknown stored suspension (deviation 11).

After them: 38 pass, 0 fail (568 `expect()` calls), about 0.6 s, `DBSIZE` 0 afterwards.

The corrupt-field mutations all fail on the attempt with no takeover flags, where the old script granted or refused instead of answering 503.

- **Third review (2026-10-31, same conditions; Redis 7.0.15 on a new random loopback port under `/tmp/vector-V05/review3/redis`, started and stopped the same way).** Three findings, all real:
  - Deviation 13 contradicted itself and 2.5, 2.7 and 2.18: its V11 clause let V11 drop a member once no run of that repository remained, while the same entry, 2.5 ("nothing else does, except the account's erasure"), 2.7's expiry and 2.18's retention allow only the account's own later bind and erasure. Fixed by deleting the clause: the one rule stands everywhere, and deviation 13 now says why the set is not trimmed (run records expire one by one and `runs:<account>` is trimmed to 200, so knowing that none remains takes a scan, and the member is a bare id of a repository the account bound itself). No code reads the clause.
  - Deviation 3's "the binding stores the token's spelling" was untested: the only letter-case case checked the outcome, and `recordRepoBinding` returns the name from TypeScript, not from the store. Confirmed: with the redeem storing `code.repository`, all 38 tests passed. Fixed with the new test "the binding holds the bind token's spelling of the repository name, never the code's" (a code spelled `octo-<id>/app`, a token spelled `Octo-<id>/App`; the returned, stored and read-back names are the token's).
  - A `binder-left` decided against a binding that was taken over, or unbound, before the suspend script ran answered `stale` or `missing` and wrote nothing, while the same suspension landing first would have kept the member through the takeover or unbind, so whether INV-P3 held for the account's 90-day run list depended on which script landed first. Confirmed with the rewritten stale-suspension test against the old module: the takeover case left the binder's set empty. Fixed in the suspend script (deviations 12 and 13, contract 2.5): with the binding read gone, a `binder-left` adds the member when the counter has passed the read generation; with another account's binding in place, it adds the member when that binding was bound after the one read (its generation, less one when suspended, is past the read one); neither path touches the binding or the counter, and other reasons stay no-ops. A later binding of the read account's own still skips it. The one ordering the store cannot see (that account's own rebind, itself gone again before the late suspension) adds the member, which hides more, never less, and is documented in deviation 13. The script now also reads the stored account with the reader's lowercase-UUID rule, and the counter with the decimal rule when the binding is gone, answering 503 before any write; on both late paths the `SADD` is the only write, so a set of another type fails the script with nothing written. The stale-suspension test now uses fresh accounts per case, adds the unbind, the unbind and another account's bind, and a takeover that was then unbound or suspended, checks that `transferred` and `deleted` write no member, and expects the member exactly in the takeover and unbind cases; it also covers a gone binding with a generation the counter never passed and another account's binding bound no later than the one read. The corrupt-data test gained the malformed or missing stored account, the corrupt counter after an unbind, and the misfiled binder-left set on the late path.

- **For later packages:**
  - V07: read a binding's `account` and `generation` through `bindingKey` and `generationKey` only. Generation equality also covers suspension (deviation 6), so V07 needs no separate suspension check.
  - V08: type `repositoryVisibility` as `"public" | "private" | "internal"`, so `GithubIdentity` stays assignable to `BindIdentity` (the test's type assertion fails otherwise).
  - V09: key the `binder-ok:<repositoryId>` cache to the binder (GitHub id) or the generation, because a takeover keeps the repository id and a cached pass for the old binder must not vouch for the new one.
  - V10: hide the repository name of every `binder-left` binding in the bindings list, whatever its stored visibility (deviation 13); validate the bind-code body's `defaultRef` as `refs/heads/<branch>` (a bare branch name answers 400); call `takeoverAllowed` for step 3, with the binding `bindingFor` returned; treat a current binder whose account is revoked as `allowanceInactive` (the mint already answers `REPO_NOT_BOUND` for it), or the repository stays unbindable for 30 days; verify `identity.ref` (INV-S11) before `redeemAndBind`; pass `peekBindCode`'s account as `account` and step 3's binder as `current`, and on `changed` re-read and decide again at most once; check the code account's own revocation; pass `deps.store` and `deps.now`. The bindings list and unbind answer 503 when a stored binding is malformed (deviation 11).
  - V11: hide the repository name, run URL and actor of every run whose repository is in the account's `binder-left:<account>` set, whatever the visibility, never only by reading the binding (an unbind or a takeover deletes it; deviation 13), and test that after a takeover and after an unbind; the erase must bump the generation of every binding it removes (so live tokens die), delete `bindings:<account>` and `binder-left:<account>`, and leave `gen:<repositoryId>`; nothing else removes a binder-left member, not even the expiry of the account's last run of that repository (2.18's retention; deviation 13); export the private key builders it needs from this module rather than writing the layout again. The INV-P3 test reads the 2.7 field list and the 2.18 bind-code row as they now stand.
  - V12: call `touchBinding` after a successful mint. Suspend with the binding read at step 6, whole (it carries the `account` the binder-left set is keyed by): `suspendBinding(binding, "transferred")` on an owner-id change there, and `suspendBinding(binding, "binder-left")` at step 9, never a fresh read, so a rebind or takeover in between is left alone (deviation 12), while a `binder-left` decided against it still reaches the account's binder-left set (deviation 13); `binder-left` from V09 stays even if a transfer follows.
  - V28: read `bindingFor(id)` and pass that binding, whole, to `suspendBinding`; for `transferred`, skip a binding whose `ownerId` already equals the payload's `repository.owner.id` (it was bound after the transfer). Replayed deliveries are then no-ops, and so are deliveries that arrive after a rebind. `renameBinding` is a no-op for an unknown repository id. Validate ids and names from the payload first (the module answers 400 for malformed ones).

### V06. Hours, slots and scheduled-runs ledger, and the gateway hook

- **Commits:** the V05 pattern. First a docs commit, `docs(github): name the V06 ledger signatures and stored fields in sections 2.4 to 2.8 and 2.18`, on top of `2ba59d90`, which changes only the contract (every contract edit listed under Files), as the section 2 preamble asks. Then this package's commit, `feat(github): V06 add the hours and slots ledger and the gateway hook`, with the module, the test, the deviations entry and this file. Both are pushed together. A commit cannot name its own hash, so the next package records both hashes, the pre-push result and the lane run. Recorded by V07: the docs commit is `af2feb9e` and the package commit `3cd4bae9`, both pushed to `origin/vector-at-mention` (still at `3cd4bae9` when V07 started). The pre-push hook's output was not kept, so no typecheck result is recorded for it.
- **Files:** `api/_lib/cloud-agents-ledger.ts` (new); `packages/web/test/cloud-agents-ledger.test.ts` (new); the contract (INV-C3's last three sentences; INV-C4's count sentence and test list; INV-S9's deadline sentence; 2.4's `thr` and `epoch` comments; 2.5's ticket bullets; 2.6 rewritten; 2.7's `live`, `used` and `sched` rows; 2.8's mint steps 13 and 14, heartbeat and settle; 2.18's agent-time and live-run rows; the V06 test list and INV-S2's and INV-S9's test lists; section 5's A18 and A59 rows); the deviations file (V06 1–14); this file (V05's commits and lane run, and this entry). No shared file is touched, `packages/engine/src/cli/vector-account.ts` is untouched, no Codium file is edited, and V05's test is unchanged. Nothing is generated. The lane is unchanged: its web step and pull-request paths already take `test/cloud-agents-*.test.ts` (V04 deviation 1), which now matches three files.
- **The module** (contract 2.6 and 2.7, deviations V06 1–13): `LiveRun`, `LedgerSource`, `LedgerPurpose`, `ReserveInput`, `ReserveResult`, `METER_EPOCH_PATTERN`, the key builders `liveKey` and `usedKey` (the `sched:<account>:<day>` builder stays private), `reserveRun`, `reportRun`, `recordCodiumRequest`, `settleRun`, `releaseRun`, `cloudAgentSecondsUsed` and `liveRun`, each one EVAL with every key in `KEYS` and a trailing `store = persistentStore`, and `CloudAgentsGatewayCaller` and `cloudAgentsGatewayHook`. It imports `GithubAgents` by relative path and only the types `CloudAgentAllowance` and `RunSource` from the entitlement seam, uses no Bun API, and never names V04's limits table. Reservations are charged to `used` up front and every settle path gives back the unused part by the one formula, and the live hash outlives every `used` key its runs are charged to, so a run nobody settles is still charged by the formula (deviation 3); a check run that replaces its own scheduled record is not counted again, and only a `counted` record is uncounted by a release (deviation 14); the six scripts share one Lua decoder, charge formula and idle rule, read and check everything before their first write, write times with `%.0f`, return numbers as decimal strings, and set expiries as durations from the caller's clock.
- **Tests** (`cloud-agents-ledger.test.ts`, 38 tests, 467 `expect()` calls, no subprocess; 33 and 405 before the first review's fixes, 37 and 434 before the second's). The harness is V05's, copied inline (deviations, "noted for reviewers"): a loopback `VECTOR_TEST_REDIS_URL` (without it, one failing test), Bun's `RedisClient` with `enableOfflineQueue: false`, an in-process `Bun.serve` fake of the REST store with the bearer check, the command log, `down`, `corrupt` and the gate, saved and restored `KV_REST_API_*` and `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_*`, fresh UUID accounts per test, no flush, and every key touched deleted in `afterAll`. Allowances are literal objects typed `Extract<CloudAgentAllowance, {active: true}>`, with each plan's row from 1.1; the clock is fixed five days in the past, which the relative expiries make safe.
  - The contract's names: "parallel reservations respect atOnce" (twenty parallel reserves through the gate on Plus, Max and Ultra give exactly 1, 2 and 6, the rest `at-once`, and `used` holds exactly those reservations); "minting again from the same check run keeps its deadline" (on a Max account with both slots full and 299 s of hours left: same start and deadline, the new `jti` stored and nothing else changed, the last heartbeat kept, `used` and agent time unchanged, the ticket consumed, the body's thread and purpose ignored for the record's, the replaced token's heartbeat and settle refused, the new one's accepted; a schedule renewal with today's count at the cap neither refused nor counted); "deadline formula and the 5-minute floor" (299 s refused and 300 s a 300-s run, with another live run's reservation, settled time, and another epoch's live run each subtracted; the longest run decides when hours are plentiful; no allowance refuses); "settle bounds and the idle window" (599 s and exactly 600 s after the last heartbeat, and after the last request, the run holds the slot; 1 ms later it is settled at 120 s and the slot is free; no activity charges 0, 1 ms rounds up to 1 s, the later activity wins either way round, a heartbeat or request after the deadline is capped at it); "a run with no heartbeats and a Codium request at +20 min, idle-settled at +31 min, is charged 20 minutes" (through the agent-time read and through another reserve); "a stale heartbeat with a recent request is not idle"; "Plus schedule refused; Max sixth schedule refused; resets at 00:00 UTC" (no key created for Plus; the sixth at 23:59:59.999 refused, comment and dispatch runs not counted, the next schedule at 00:00:00.000 counted on the new day; the count expires 2 days after its last write); "a scheduled check run that mints again after a generation change is counted once" (the fifth Max schedule, stored with `counted: true`, mints again after a rebind and is neither refused at the cap nor counted again, its replacement holds no `counted`, releasing the replacement leaves the count at five and a new sixth schedule is still refused; a schedule started at 23:59 and minted again at 00:01 is counted on its first day only, and so is a third mint that finds its own record idle and settles it first); "new epoch starts fresh"; "the gateway hook records run requests and ignores other callers".
  - Also: a fresh reserve is one EVAL with three declared keys, writes exactly 2.6's record (also read back through `liveRun`), charges 2700 s up front with agent time still 0, and sets `live` and `used` to expire the period's end plus 40 days from the ledger's clock; a past period end keeps `used` 40 days; eight parallel first mints of one check run (comment and schedule) give one fresh reservation and seven renewals, one record holding one of their `jti`s, one reservation charged and one schedule counted; a generation change settles the old run at 600 s and reserves fresh, and the older generation cannot take it back; the ticket survives `at-once`, `hours` and `scheduled` refusals, is declared as the fourth key, and is consumed by a reservation and by a renewal; six parallel mints from different check runs with one ticket, on Ultra's six slots, reserve once and the other five answer `refusal: "ticket"`, the holder still renews without it, eight parallel mints of one check run carrying a ticket give one reservation and seven renewals, and a consumed or never-stored ticket is refused before the slots and writes nothing; a re-mint at and after the deadline answers `at-once` and leaves the record, the `jti` and the ticket alone, the earlier token still reports and settles, and one millisecond before the deadline a re-mint renews; the four settle paths (explicit, the reserve's sweep, the agent-time read's sweep, a generation change) charge the same 541 s for one record; `settleRun` needs the holding token, is idempotent, keeps the whole reservation for another epoch's token and creates nothing for an unknown run; the live record is kept as long as the agent time its runs are charged to, a run of a later-ending period lengthens it, heartbeats, requests, settles, releases, sweeps and a reserve whose period has ended leave it, a 50-day run keeps it until its deadline plus a day, and the hash goes with its last run; heartbeats move forward only, stay live past the deadline until settled, and create nothing; requests keep the latest, ignore one before the start, and do nothing after a settle, for an unknown run or for another account; a release after a failed fresh mint charges 0 and frees the slot and the scheduled run, a release naming another `jti`, start or epoch or made after a renewal changes nothing, and a renewal is refused at the type level and with 400; a schedule reserved at 23:59:59.900 UTC and released at 00:00:00.100 uncounts its start's day and leaves the next day's count, which holds another schedule, alone; an idle run of the old epoch frees its slot for the new one and keeps its whole reservation in its own epoch, and so does a run of the old epoch replaced after a generation change (2700 s kept, not the formula's 300); an agent-time read for another epoch leaves the current epoch's idle run to that epoch's own settle (300 s); agent time counts lazily settled runs and leaves out live reservations, and Codium's two-argument call works; a run nobody settles (cancelled at +2 min, or its token killed by a rebind, an unbind or a suspension) outlives its agent time's expiry and is charged the formula's 120 s, not its 1800-s reservation, by an agent-time read and by a reserve 20 days later; odd-millisecond and sixteen-digit times survive the scripts exactly.
  - Fail closed (INV-S16): with the store down and with an unrecognised reply, all eight calls reject with 503 and nothing changes; 29 malformed live records (an unknown or missing field, null, string, fractional, zero or negative times, a deadline before the start or not whole seconds after it, activity before the start, a bad epoch, source, purpose, thread, repository id or `jti`, `counted` on a comment run or as `false` or `1`, a bad generation, not JSON, an array, a string, null) answer 503 from all eight readers and write nothing; one malformed run refuses the four calls that read every run and leaves heartbeats and requests on the healthy run working; six malformed `used` values, three malformed `sched` values and `used` below a give-back answer 503 with nothing written; a live, used or scheduled key of another type refuses exactly the calls that read it; 43 invalid reserve inputs and 24 invalid inputs to the other functions answer 400 with no store command; each function sends exactly one EVAL.
  - Types (checked by `packages/web`'s typecheck): `LiveRun` equals 2.6's shape, `counted?: true` included; the hook is a `GatewayHook` and its `afterSettle` a plain `(ModelPlanCaller, AfterSettleContext) => Promise<void>` value, against a local copy of Codium's types at `origin/codium` `99fcf1c0`; `cloudAgentSecondsUsed` fits Codium's two-argument call; a renewed reservation does not compile as `releaseRun`'s argument.
