- **Left to CI:** the ledger test in the lane's one-process web step (after `github-oidc`, `github-app`, `github-app-api` and V05's binding test) against Valkey 7.2.14; the lane policy and `api-module-loading` on a clean runner. No CI job reaches Upstash: how its REST API answers these scripts (RESP2 replies, declared keys, `PEXPIRE` with a duration) is first exercised by the owner's acceptance run (section 4), and so is the hook inside Codium's real gateway (A59).
- **CI:** V06 changes no workflow. The lane `vector-at-mention.yml` runs by itself on the push of these two commits. That run is pending when this is written, and the next package records it. (Resolved: see run `41174152350`.) Nothing else is dispatched: the coordinator dispatches full CI per phase. Recorded by V07, from the GitHub API's job list for the run: lane run `41174152350` (attempt 1, push of `3cd4bae9`, 2026-10-31 05:50 to 05:51 UTC) completed with `success` in all three jobs, `hosted API` (`124458111590`: the hosted API typecheck, the GitHub API tests step with the binding, entitlement and ledger tests, the hosted API tests step and the lane policy step, each `success`), `engine and core` (`124458111342`) and `desktop engine bundle (node)` (`124458111545`). V07 did not read the job logs, so no test counts are recorded from CI.
- **For later packages:**
  - V07: read live runs through `liveKey` only, and require the record to exist and its `jti` to equal the token's (`usedKey` is exported but V07 needs no agent time). A record may carry `counted: true` (deviation 14), so read it through this module's rules or read only its `jti`, never with a stricter copy of the field list. Validate the `epoch` claim with `METER_EPOCH_PATTERN`. An idle run that no reserve has swept yet is still live, and its record can stay up to 40 days past the period's end (deviation 3); the token's own `dl` and `exp` bound it.
  - V11: the INV-P3 test reads 2.18's live-run and agent-time rows as they now stand; `live:<account>`, `used:<account>:<epoch>` and `sched:<account>:<day>` are the ledger's keys, and erase must remove all three. The epochs and days are not named by any one key, so V11 needs a way to find them (a scan of the account's prefix, or an index it adds here first); they expire by themselves after at most 40 days past the period's end and 2 days, and `live:<account>` no sooner than the agent time its runs are charged to (or a run's deadline plus a day, when later). 2.18's live-run row now gives that retention.
  - V12: map source and purpose as 2.8 step 10 does (purpose `scheduled` exactly for `github-schedule`, or the reserve answers 400); sign `epoch`, `thr`, `src`, `pur` and `dl` from `reserveRun`'s answer, never from the body or the allowance (a renewal answers the record's); on a failure after the reserve call `releaseRun(account, rid, {...answer, jti})` only when `answer.renewed` is false; pass the claims to `reportRun` and `settleRun`; pass `deps.store` and `deps.now`. Every `ok` answer's deadline is after `now`: a re-mint at or after the record's deadline answers `at-once` and leaves the earlier token working (deviation 7), so no `dl ≤ now` check is needed. Map `{ok: false, refusal: "ticket"}` to 403 `ACTOR_NOT_ALLOWED` and a `limit` refusal to 429 `MODEL_PLAN_RUN_LIMIT {limit}`, and pass `consumeKey` only for the ticket run step 8 accepted (deviation 13).
  - V13: a mint that fails after the reserve has already consumed the one-time ticket (the reserve deletes it on success, before `mintRunToken`), so the job's retry finds no ticket at step 8; release gives back the reservation but cannot restore the ticket. The reserve is also where a ticket is single-use: of parallel mints from different check runs that peeked one ticket, exactly one reserves and the others answer `refusal: "ticket"` (deviation 13), so "the ticket is single-use" holds for parallel mints too.
  - A18 and A59: `cloudAgentSecondsUsed(accountId, meterEpoch)` answers settled seconds of that epoch, settles only that epoch's idle runs, and fails closed with 503; keep exporting `CloudAgentAllowance` and `RunSource` from the entitlement seam.

### V07. Run tokens and the verifier seam

- **Commits:** the V05/V06 pattern. First a docs commit, `docs(github): name the V07 run-token signatures, claim rules and errors in section 2.4`, on top of `3cd4bae9`, which changes only the contract (every contract edit listed under Files), as the section 2 preamble asks. Then this package's commit, `feat(github): V07 add run tokens and the verifier seam`, with the module, both tests, the deviations entry and this file. Both are pushed together. A commit cannot name its own hash, so the next package records both hashes, the pre-push result and the lane run. Shared files touched: none. `packages/engine/src/cli/vector-account.ts`, `api/_lib/revocation.ts`, `api/_lib/cli-token.ts` and every Codium file are untouched, and the binding and ledger modules are unchanged. Recorded by V08: the docs commit is `bd131c3e` and the package commit `8542c70d`, both pushed to `origin/vector-at-mention` (still at `8542c70d` when V08 started, with a clean worktree). The pre-push hook's output was not kept, so no typecheck result is recorded for it.
- **Files:** `api/_lib/cloud-agents-run-token.ts` (new); `packages/web/test/cloud-agents-run-token.test.ts` (new); `test/cli-token.test.ts` (one new case); the contract (2.4 rewritten with the signatures, claim rules, the check's keys and the errors; the V07 test list; section 5's A16 row); the deviations file (V07 1 to 9); this file (V06's commits and lane run, and this entry). Nothing is generated. The lane is unchanged: its web step and pull-request paths already take `test/cloud-agents-*.test.ts`, which now matches four files, and its hosted API step already runs `cli-token.test.ts`.
- **The module** (contract 2.4, deviations V07 1 to 9): `RunTokenClaims`, `RunCaller`, `RunTokenDeps`, `mintRunToken`, `readRunToken`, `verifyRunToken`, `requireRunToken` and `cloudAgentsRunVerifier`. It uses `node:crypto` and `effect` only, no Bun API, and imports other files by relative `.js` paths: `GithubAgents`, `bindingKey` and `generationKey`, `liveKey`, `METER_EPOCH_PATTERN` and the ledger's source and purpose types, `cloudAgentRevokedBefore` (a value, so A59's body swap takes effect) and `RunSource`. The key is a dedicated secret of at least 32 characters, or HMAC-SHA256 of a license secret of at least 32 characters with the label `vector-run-token-key-v1`. Claims are read with one Effect Schema struct that refuses excess properties, plus the relations between fields. Verification is one read-only EVAL with four declared keys (the binding, the generation counter, the account's live runs, the revocation mark) and one `cloudAgentRevokedBefore` call, started together. Every refusal is one 401 with one message, and every failure one 503.
- **Tests** (`cloud-agents-run-token.test.ts`, 22 tests, 241 `expect()` calls after the review fixes below, 221 before; no subprocess). The format, key and domain tests are pure (8 tests); the rest use V05's and V06's harness, copied inline, with one route more (`revokeAccountTokens`' path-style `SET`) and the run-token check sent to the store as `EVAL_RO` (deviations, the note). Bindings, reservations, renewals, settles, suspensions and revocations go through the real modules; allowances are Max's row as a literal. Secrets and store variables are saved and restored, and every key touched is deleted in `afterAll`.
  - The contract's names: "domain separation both ways" (with the run and CLI secrets set equal: a run token is refused by `verifyCliToken`, `verifyDesktopToken` and `requireModelPlanUser`, which makes no store request, and so is `vct_` over its body; a CLI token, a desktop token, a deletion grant and `vrt_` over each of their bodies are refused by `readRunToken`; a run payload under `vector-cli-v1` is refused and under `vector-run-v1` accepted; a CLI payload under `vector-run-v1` is refused by `verifyCliToken` and under `vector-cli-v1` accepted); "rebind, unbind, settle, replacement, revocation and revokedBefore each invalidate" (also a takeover and a suspension, each shown accepted first, on the gateway path and both service paths; the key `revokeAccountTokens` wrote is the one the check declared, and `requireUnrevokedAccount` agrees; `revokedBefore` at `iat` + 1 refuses, at `iat`, 0 and none passes); "the replaced token is refused" (after a renewal the old token is refused and the new one accepted; with a heartbeat a minute before the deadline, a re-mint at the deadline answers `at-once`, and the token that holds the run still passes the service path at `dl` and `exp` − 1, is refused at `exp`, is refused at the gateway at `dl`, and settles); "payload has no email" (exactly 2.4's fourteen names in order, no `@`, and an email, plan, limits, model or `premiumModels` claim refused at run time and, for the email, at compile time); "caller satisfies the registry contract" (a token minted from a reservation made now, verified by `cloudAgentsRunVerifier.verify` with the default clock, store and seam, checked against every registry rule and, with `satisfies`, against a local copy of Codium's `ModelPlanCaller`, with `prefix` a `RunPrefix` and `verify` a `RunVerifier`).
  - Also: the known answer (the signature is HMAC-SHA256(key, `vector-run-v1.` + payload) as base64url, a hand-built payload in another key order reads the same, and six other domains, none included, are refused); 20 malformed or tampered tokens; the 1024-character limit checked before any HMAC; 44 invalid claims refused by the reader and 41 of them by the mint (the other three are the mint's own `v` and `exp`); the longest valid claims (767 characters); the key derivation (license only, the derived key pinned, the raw license secret and the CLI token key refused, a dedicated secret of 31 and of exactly 32, a license secret of 32) and five unusable configurations, which answer 503 with no store or seam call; "deadline versus exp on the two paths" (`dl` − 1 and `dl`, `exp` − 1 and `exp`, a clock that is not a number, an `allowAfterDeadline` that is not `true`); one message for each of eight refusal causes and six invalid-token causes; only an exact `Bearer` header is read; one EVAL with exactly the four keys and the four arguments, and one seam call, per verification, and none for a refused token; the EVAL and the seam call start in parallel; a store outage, an unknown reply, six unknown answers, a store rejection and a store that throws answer 503 and change nothing; 14 kinds of corrupt or foreign stored data answer 503 and change nothing; nine kinds of seam failure answer 503; each of these logs exactly one line per call with its cause and the run id, never the account or the failure's text, and a refusal logs none; the EVAL's answer is reported before the seam's; 2.4's shapes, at the type level.
  - `test/cli-token.test.ts` › "a run token is refused by verifyCliToken": a run token minted from the license secret alone (the test removes `VECTOR_RUN_TOKEN_SECRET` and restores it), and `vct_` over its body, are refused by `verifyCliToken` and `verifyDesktopToken` with 401 `CLI_TOKEN_INVALID`.
- **Local evidence (2026-10-31, Bun 1.3.14, each through `vector-serial`, one file at a time, `GITHUB_ACTIONS=false`, `XDG_*`, `VECTOR_TEST_HOME` and `TMPDIR` under `/tmp/vector-V07`):**

A scratch probe under `/tmp/vector-V07/probe` confirmed beforehand: `TYPE` of a missing key is `none`; `HMGET` of a missing hash answers `false` for each field; `pcall(cjson.decode)` fails on `{`, gives a table for `[1]` and a string for `"x"`; `HGET` on a string raises `WRONGTYPE`; `EVAL_RO` runs a reading script and refuses a write. (1) A store passed in deps that throws synchronously escaped `requireLive` as its raw error, because `store([...])` ran while the `Promise.allSettled` array was being built; a new case in "store outage fails closed" reproduced it before the fix (all three paths answered the raw `TypeError`, not 503), and the EVAL now starts inside `Promise.resolve().then(...)`, as the seam does. (2) Every failure was one 503 with no trace of its cause, so corrupt stored data could not be told from an outage; `requireLive` now logs one `console.warn("Vector cloud-agents run token check failed", {cause, runId})` per failure, with no account and no error text. No guard on the run id is needed, unlike `logLedgerMiss`: the rid comes from a decoded, well-signed token, so it already matches the `gh-` grammar. The four tests that reach a 503 capture the lines with `spyOn(console, "warn")` (the ledger test's pattern) and compare them exactly. Evidence: `packages/web` `test/cloud-agents-run-token.test.ts` with `VECTOR_TEST_REDIS_URL`: 22 pass, 0 fail (241 `expect()` calls), `DBSIZE` 0 afterwards. Six mutations of the fix, each on the backup `/tmp/vector-V07/review/backup/cloud-agents-run-token.ts` through `/tmp/vector-V07/review/mutate/run.py`, which restores the module and compares it with the backup: the store called synchronously again (only "store outage fails closed" fails); `corrupt` logged as `store` (only the corrupt-data test); an unknown answer logged as `corrupt` (only the outage test); a seam value logged as `seam rejected` (only the seam test); no log line (the four 503 tests); the account logged in place of the rid (the outage and ordering tests). The module matched the backup afterwards.

(1) The equal-secret test alone could not prove the `vector-run-v1` domain, because mint and read share one signing function; the known-answer test now does, and the "no domain prefix" and "CLI domain" mutations fail it. The reverse case now signs a CLI-shaped payload under the run domain, so only the domain differs. (2) INV-S9 names a V07 test, "the replaced token is refused", which now exists, with the at-deadline re-mint case. (3) Every refusal now shares one error and one message (deviation 8), and a test compares them. (4) A license secret under 32 characters gives no key, as in `cli-token.ts` (deviation 3), and the boundaries are tested.

### V08. OIDC verifier: `mint` and `bind` audiences, job-workflow claims, review comments, the App bot

- **Commits:** the V05 to V07 pattern. First a docs commit, `docs(github): name the V08 identity fields, claim rules and refusal codes in section 2.10`, on top of `8542c70d`, which changes only the contract (every contract edit listed under Files), as the section 2 preamble asks. Then this package's commit, `feat(github): V08 verify mint and bind OIDC tokens`, with the module, the test, the deviations entry and this file. The session that built V08 left the tree uncommitted, as it was asked; a separate gate session checked `git status` (only the five files under Files changed, nothing untracked) and that no source or test file changed after the last passing run, then made both commits and the push. A commit cannot name its own hash, so the next package records both hashes, the pre-push result and the lane run. Shared files touched: none. `packages/engine/src/cli/vector-account.ts`, `api/_lib/github-api.ts`, `api/_lib/github-app.ts`, `packages/web/test/github-app-fixture.ts` and every Codium file are untouched.
- **Files:** `api/_lib/github-oidc.ts`; `packages/web/test/github-oidc.test.ts` (15 new tests and four new imported names: one new import, `GithubAgents`, and `consumeGithubOidc`, `type GithubIdentity` and `type GithubOidcPurpose` added to the existing `github-oidc` import; no existing line changed); the contract (2.10: the identity type, the subject forms, the added-claims rule, the refusal codes in check order and what `actorIsBot: false` means; the V08 test list; V09's author-type check and its test, and that test under INV-S2); the deviations file (V08 1 to 8); this file (V07's commits and lane run, and this entry). Nothing is generated. The lane is unchanged: its web step already runs `test/github-oidc.test.ts` first, and its pull-request paths already take `api/**` and `packages/web/test/**`.
- **The module** (contract 2.10, deviations V08 1 to 8): `githubOidcAudiences` gains `mint` and `bind` from `GithubAgents.AUDIENCE` (imported by its relative `.js` path, as the cloud-agents modules do); each audience has its own event list (`token` and `installation` keep the four, `mint` adds `pull_request_review_comment`, `bind` takes `workflow_dispatch` only). The job-workflow claims pass only when both are absent, or both are strings equal to `workflow_ref` and `workflow_sha`, on every audience; `environment` stays refused. A review comment, on `mint` only, needs a `refs/pull/<n>/merge` ref, a `pull_request` subject in either form, branch-shaped `head_ref` and `base_ref`, and `ref_type` `branch` or absent. A `<slug>[bot]` actor passes only on a mint dispatch, with `actorIsBot: true`. The identity gains `actorIsBot`, `jobWorkflowRef`, `runnerEnvironment` (required on `mint`), `refProtected`, `repositoryVisibility`, and, for a review comment, `pullRequest`, `headRef` and `baseRef`. Codes and order are today's. `consumeGithubOidc` is unchanged. It uses `node:crypto` and `jose` only, no Bun API.
- **Tests** (`github-oidc.test.ts`: 41 tests, 386 `expect()` calls, of which the 26 existing tests are unchanged and 15 are new; no subprocess, no environment change, no Redis; the file's one loopback JWKS server and its `afterAll` are reused). Each case signs its claims for the audience it is verified against, and each list of cases is compared as one object of label to outcome, so a failure names its case.
  - The contract's names (V08 test list): "equal job-workflow claims are accepted on every audience" (`token`, `installation`, `mint`, `bind` and a mint review comment; `jobWorkflowRef` recorded, `undefined` without the claims; equal claims at an evil workflow path or another commit still answer `WORKFLOW_NOT_TRUSTED`); "unequal or lone job-workflow claims are refused on every audience" (ref alone, sha alone, a reusable workflow, another sha, a non-string ref, a `null` sha, and a mismatch beside an evil path, all 401); "environment is refused on every audience" (named, empty, not a string); "mint accepts a review comment, and token still refuses it" (both subject forms, `ref_type` absent, `refs/pull/9999999999/merge` with `pullRequest` 9999999999, a 240-character `head_ref`; the same claims re-signed for `token`, `installation` and `bind` answer 401; on `mint`, ten ref and subject refusals answer 401 and thirteen head, base, `ref_type` and workflow refusals answer 403); "events other than review comments keep ref_type branch and no head or base ref on every audience" (critique item 1); "token and installation keep their four events, and refuse a review comment at the event list" (critique item 2); "bind accepts only workflow_dispatch"; "the App bot is accepted only on mint for workflow_dispatch" (`vector-fixture[bot]`, `github-actions[bot]`, `0[bot]` and 99 characters before `[bot]` accepted; ### V11. Run records and the stored-data list

- Contract `f6cbdcbe` defines record ownership, generation/token comparisons, the public optional fields used for privacy, retention, erasure ordering and the account-attributable binder cache. V10's independent fixture-policy correction is `e10ec8f2`. This implementation follows them in its own commit; the next package records the push hook and scoped CI. None has been pushed yet at this writing. (Resolved: V11's `5b638f0e` is the head of run `41260087750`, which failed one run-token fixture test; first all-green run `41260931699`.)
- Implemented `api/_lib/cloud-agents-runs.ts`, its HTTP route and the API's account-authenticated `runs` handler. Start checks the current binding, generation, live reservation and token id atomically; end compares account/generation/token before its idempotent outcome/verdict update. A later token or account replacement cannot be overwritten by an earlier start/settle. Listings read records, actors and privacy sets in one snapshot, filter by owning account, retain at most 200 indexed runs, and hide names/URLs/actors after binder-left even following unbind or takeover.
- The account eraser checks the existing revocation marker first, then removes owned bindings, indexes, codes, tickets, ledgers, records/actors (including history trimmed from the index) and attributable binder caches. It keeps generation counters and shared public default-branch metadata. Shared records are deleted conditionally on their current stored account, so another account's replacement survives.
- Binder caches now include server-derived account identity along with GitHub id and generation. Legacy entries never authorize from cache; fresh GitHub checks rewrite successful decisions. Legacy entries without account identity cannot be attributed during erasure and only retain their existing bounded expiry. A malformed/non-expiring legacy entry fails closed. The production acceptance gate must start with the new format or wait out the legacy hour.
- Independent read-only review found and then verified fixes for delayed cache writes and binder-left suspensions recreating data after erasure. Code issuance has the same fence. The final scripts check revocation before code issuance/redemption, suspension, binder-cache creation and run-record writes. The reviewer found no remaining code blocker after re-reading those fixes, the delayed-writer regressions, legacy validation and exact inventory assertions.
- Preliminary local checks passed the run-record file (17 cases before the added migration/race cases), App cache tests (61 cases), binding library (39), binding API (13), entitlement seam (18), and hosted API types. The first run-record test failed only because its raw-HGETALL helper assumed Bun returned an array; reading that raw response through EVAL corrected the fixture.   - The root cause (deviation V18c item 2): on Bun 1.3.14, once `process.stdin.isTTY` has been read, `for await` over a stdin that Bun.spawn was given as bytes ends with none of them. Reproduced here (`/tmp/vector-v18c/node-probe/bun-parent.ts`, through the lock): the old reader read 0 of 401 bytes given to Bun.spawn as bytes, and 401 of 401 through an ended node:child_process pipe (the sandbox's way); the new reader loaded the channel both ways and left stdin destroyed. Under Node v22.23.4 (`parent.mjs`, `parent-old.mjs` in the same folder) the new reader loaded the channel and refused an empty stdin at once, and Node's own `for await` read all 401 bytes.

### Resume integration with main (2026-11-09)

- Base: `vector-at-mention` at `15069641b7940ddabcfd3c49d921984505c089f7` (V18c).
- Main: `0dcbb8e2d1cc01d22d4b98695d651430f10f1d2c`, the merged multiagent, self-evolving and Codium A00–A28 build.
- Work resumes in a fresh worktree; the earlier incomplete local source remains untouched. This merge does not install the v3 workflow, activate a hosted service or publish a CLI.
- Three conflicts were resolved:
  - `index.ts`: keep main's owner/workflow and local-policy command gate, with the GitHub channel loaded before account verification and the four control commands excluded from that verification.
  - `vector-account.ts`: keep the channel-backed credentials alongside main's browser-login nonce and token-purpose changes.
  - `session/retry.ts`: retain main's separate accounting of concurrency waits and provider failures while preserving the GitHub run's pinned stop and wait policy. A wait under the pinned rule also leaves the ordinary failure retry budget intact.
- The merge also brings main's Codium evidence projection and review cost labels. V19 builds on these rather than replacing them with the earlier reducer.
- Added regression: twelve pinned gateway waits followed by ten ordinary provider-failure retries still allow all ten failures before stopping on the next one.
- Validation: the frozen dependency install passed. Independent source review cleared all three conflict resolutions and twelve files changed by both branches that merged automatically. Focused runtime checks cover 100 distinct passing cases: retry 41, command gate 5, agent Codium: 10, agent phase 16, workflow compliance 28. The new retry fixture initially used a boolean where the metadata schema requires a string; correcting that fixture made all 41 retry cases pass. The initial Codium file passed nine cases and found one stale expectation: main now stops unpinned run-limit, daily-limit and model-unavailable responses immediately. The corrected retry case passed separately; these are ten distinct cases, not an added full rerun. Each file ran separately with four fresh XDG roots and a 30-second test timeout.
- The mandatory pre-push hook and exact-head lane CI remain pending. The previous V18c exact-head run `42465949815` remains red at its CLI credential-output assertion; that repair follows this merge as a separate source checkpoint. No full CI success, live owner acceptance or release is claimed.

### V18c output-boundary credential repair (2026-11-10)

- The existing exact-entrypoint regression in run `42465949815` failed because tool arguments containing synthetic credentials were printed verbatim. Child environment isolation already passed; the log path bypassed the private credential holder after its values were deliberately removed from `process.env`.
- The holder now owns a redactor for literal and JSON-escaped credential values, including multiline key fragments. The agent applies it to its logs, errors, tool/model event output and bundle title/body/comment. Raw session history and patch bytes stay intact. Legacy helper callers retain their existing output channels and blank-line behavior.
- Independent review found another output path in attachment match/download diagnostics. The agent now supplies its filtered callbacks there too. The regression uses an attachment URL that is rejected locally, so it exercises both diagnostics without contacting GitHub.
- Focused checks passed: private credential/HTTP boundary 41 cases, one real in-process phase case covering tool/model/attachment logs and bundle text while proving raw history remains intact, and one existing own-key lifecycle case proving no heartbeat/settle. The first typecheck found two fixture issues (missing issue body and a partial holder lacking the new redactor); the fixture now uses the real holder. Final engine types, formatting and the diff check passed. Independent source review cleared the repair after the attachment boundary correction. The mandatory push hook and exact-entrypoint CI remain pending at this checkpoint.
- V19 and V21 have isolated drafts outside this worktree. They are not part of this repair or accepted completion evidence.

### Exact-entrypoint token output follow-up (2026-11-10)

- PR #25 run `42743954090` passed 372 engine/core cases and failed the existing actual CLI credential-output assertion. The remaining value was `vrt_fixture-agent.signature`, which the fixture places in model tool text but never supplies to its own-key credential channel. That mode rejects run-token credentials. Every credential actually held by the channel was filtered; this result does not show a held run token escaping the private holder.
- Public output now also withholds a complete `vct_` or `vrt_` token literal from another source, using the existing canonical schemas and length limits. Both the structured-token detector and held-value detector match the original input, then their overlapping spans are replaced together. Either replacement order alone can break a match when one credential contains or overlaps another token.
- Added narrow grammar, JSON/multiline and overlap regressions. The actual in-process phase now includes the same foreign run-token literal and a foreign CLI token, while still requiring raw history to retain the original values. The actual CLI assertion is unchanged. Patch bytes remain outside this output filter.
- Parent source review is clear. The final combined repair tree passed the private-cloud file (43 cases, one Windows-only skip) and the real in-process phase case (one case, 16 unrelated cases filtered). The latter retains the same foreign literal as the CI-only entrypoint assertion, which is unchanged and awaits exact-head CI. Engine typecheck passed. The clean V19 checkpoint is `4312f596e`; this correction is a separate checkpoint before V20.

### V20 Codium review lifecycle (2026-11-12, source checkpoint)

### V13 Everywhere routing preparation port (2026-11-12)

- `prepareCloudRun` validates the existing binding and allowance and issues a private one-time ticket without dispatching Actions. It returns only the ticket hash and server-side `dispatch`/`cancel` closures. This lets Everywhere atomically choose the Actions route before any workflow can start; a losing laptop/Actions race cancels the unused ticket. Existing `startCloudRun` composes preparation and dispatch.
- Dispatch is single-operation/idempotent within the preparation and rechecks expiry, current binding/generation/suspension, App availability, current allowance, revocation and the exact unconsumed ticket before the App request. Source gates remain unchanged. A cancelled preparation cannot dispatch; cancellation after dispatch starts cannot revoke a run that won the route.
- ROOT's hosted API typecheck passes. Real Redis fixture coverage for preparation, cancellation, concurrent dispatch and late authorization changes is added to `cloud-agents-start.test.ts`; it remains CI-only and has not run at this checkpoint. No Actions fallback is activated by this source change.

### V21 trusted publisher (2026-11-12, source checkpoint)

- `vector github publish` is registered without a project instance. It independently derives routing from the trusted route job, event, current checkout and actual PR metadata; an invalid bundle, extra entry or mismatched echo cannot acquire a publishing credential. Its reads are bounded, link-refusing fixed bundle filenames. Repository configuration, plugins and instructions never configure or execute in the publisher.
- Patch validation holds back GitHub workflow/action paths independently of the bundle, including case/Windows path aliases; refuses traversal, Git metadata, gitlinks and escaping links; and bounds binary literal/delta expansion before Git can apply it. The final index receives another mode/path/blob-size audit. Only `git apply --index` supplies committed paths; the publisher never stages the checkout with `git add`, installs dependencies, runs checks or executes repository hooks/filters.
- Publishing defaults to `GITHUB_TOKEN`. Inline review comments, server-started bot dispatches and non-writer thread content retain that credential even with App opt-in. An eligible App publication uses the existing exact `publish` permission profile and disposes the credential on completion or failure. Model text cannot forge markers, close another issue or notify an account; the publisher adds its own issue close, run marker and CI caveat.
- Pushes create a fresh `vector/…` branch or update only a same-repository bot-owned Vector PR head at the unchanged source commit. A moved or human head receives a separate draft; a fork never grants push authority. Owned PR metadata gets the new run/evidence, existing drafts remain drafts and any required downgrade converts a ready PR to a draft. Failed or uncertain pushes never overwrite a changed head.
- ROOT validation: the final publisher file passes **21 cases, 125 assertions**, using actual Git repositories and bare remotes; the unchanged authentication file passes **33 cases, 209 assertions**; engine typecheck passes. Coverage includes binary round-trip/expansion guards, held-back action/workflow files, forged echoes, extra/plugin bundles, actual advanced remote heads, default-branch protection, draft conservatism, no-change/refusal dedupe, credential cleanup and the project-plugin sentinel. Exact published-entrypoint/hosted CI, schedules (V22), remaining setup surfaces and live owner acceptance are still pending. No customer repository or production service was changed by validation.

### V22 configured scheduled runs (2026-11-12, source checkpoint)

- Exact default-branch schedule configuration supplies the task prompt, stable id and cron-derived conversation thread. Unknown, ambiguous, malformed/prototype or impossible-calendar maps refuse before model work. The actual in-process agent path runs a configured task and records a schedule bundle; unconfigured Codium schedules settle as refused. Legacy v2 callers without the schedule map retain their existing prompt path.
- Shared `cronFor` converts daily/weekday/weekly local wall times to UTC, including half-hour offsets and weekday rollover, and supplies the explicit ±1 hour daylight-saving note. One-shot recurrences are refused. Minute spreading preserves distinct stable ids; ids reject control/newline suffixes. Generated workflow configuration validates calendar bounds and caps the combined escaped schedule environment value at 120,000 bytes.
- Publishing creates `vector/schedule-<id>-<run>-<attempt>` or refreshes a same-repository bot-owned open proposal for that exact stable id. Reuse requires the complete numeric run/attempt suffix, preventing a `daily` schedule from taking a `daily-123` schedule's branch. Fresh API and fetched Git heads must agree. The resulting validated tree contains the current default branch plus this run's patch, with the previous proposal and current default as commit parents; prior proposal history remains reachable without executing merge tools or repository code. Existing drafts remain drafts. Human/fork/other-id proposals grant no reuse authority.
- ROOT's final source checks pass: Core and engine types; Core cron **5 cases/31 assertions**; engine lookup **2/11**; publisher **24/149** using actual Git; configured/unconfigured actual agent paths **2/12**. The unchanged generated-workflow checks pass **35 cases/721 assertions/3 snapshots** and route-script checks **17/529**. Exact-head hosted CI, published CLI entrypoint and live GitHub schedule/PR acceptance remain pending.

### V27 authenticated artifact codec (2026-11-12, source checkpoint)

- The pure Node-compatible conversation codec implements the previously recorded bounded VSA1 routing header, exact repository/thread/run AAD and AES-256-GCM authentication before any ZIP parsing. The fixed manifest separates immutable desktop-approved initial Brain bytes from run-origin proposal drafts and caps their combined bytes. Wrong authority, corrupted/expired/unavailable storage and disallowed Brain-derived history fall back to the live GitHub thread. A different fresh Brain digest cannot recover an older Brain-derived conversation.
- Both ZIP entry declarations and actual streaming expansion are bounded; entries are read in memory without extraction. GitHub artifact selection reconstructs API URLs from the exact repository/id, selects the newest matching unexpired conversation artifact and removes repository authorization on narrowly allowed signed storage redirects. The desktop's explicit owner reader can additionally bind decryption to the exact owner-authorized run id.
- ROOT's final permanent file passes **8 cases/42 assertions**, and engine typecheck passes. Actual crypto/ZIP round trips found a zip.js ownership assumption: its reader slices input before constructing a DataView without a byte offset, while Node Buffer slicing returns a view. Inputs now become owned Uint8Arrays, including a permanent nonzero-offset Buffer regression; split/multidisk, integrity and expansion refusals are unchanged. Temporary diagnostic exports/tests were removed after the original cases passed.
- This checkpoint qualifies the codec only. Runtime save/restore, the exact-byte private runner attestation, initial Brain download/context use, returned-proposal collection, desktop owner preview/import, actual hosted storage and integrated CI remain pending. Public privacy wording is not switched to an encrypted-artifact promise until those boundaries are verified. New Brain routes remain off.

It also fails locally at this head (1 fail, run with `-t` only), so it is deterministic and not specific to the PR merge commit. A local diagnostic shows the wrapper plugin's `onResolve` in `packages/engine/script/sandbox-bun-undici.ts` does match: the resolved real path equals the wrapper source, so the plugin returns `{ path: "./assets/platform-node-undici.mjs", external: true }`. Yet Bun 1.3.14's output keeps the original specifier. The pin was already 1.3.14 when the wrapper landed (`a3b15235`). The probe-bun bundle therefore never gets the preserved wrapper, which matches the sandbox job's "Cannot find module './Undici.js'". Next step for whoever fixes it: confirm whether Bun 1.3.14 drops the `path` of an external `onResolve` result and rewrite the import another way. Until that is fixed, the lane's engine-and-core job and the (codium, success, bun) sandbox job cannot be green on this branch, so BT01's "lane jobs green" done criterion is blocked on it. This needs its own package, which the lead should schedule.

### BT03 B08 Brain upload store hardening against real Redis (2026-11-15, branch brain-transport)

- **What changed.** Three server fixes, all behind `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` (off), each with a case that fails at `ad3928f7`. Reasons, alternatives and effects: deviations, "2026-11-15 BT03".
  - `api/_lib/cloud-agents-brain.ts`, `UPDATE` script: a commit, bind or read sets `PX min(expiresAt - now, PTTL(key))`. A writer with a slower clock can no longer extend an upload past its two hours and past the account index that erasure reads. A key without an expiry (`PTTL` -1) is `corrupt` (503); a `PTTL` of 0, a live key's last millisecond, is `stale` (400), like an upload past its `expiresAt`. No export or signature changes.
  - `api/_lib/cloud-agents-desktop.ts`: one gate wraps the prepare, commit, download and desktop-run routes. While the switch is off, each answers its fixed 503 `BRAIN_UPLOAD_UNAVAILABLE` before any method, origin, body, credential, limit or store work. The return keys `{prepare, commit, download, start}` and the factory's input are unchanged.
  - `api/_lib/cloud-agents-api.ts`, `artifactKey`: after the per-run limit it reads the live run. Another `jti` or a missing run is 401 `MODEL_PLAN_RUN_EXPIRED`, and a live `triggerSource` of `desktop` is 403 `ARTIFACT_KEY_NOT_ALLOWED`, so an owner-desktop run never holds the ordinary conversation key.
  - `cloud-agents-brain-api.ts`, `cloud-agents-owner-artifact.ts` and `cloud-agents-runs.ts` are unchanged (no defect found).
  - Spec 2.7 (`brain:<uploadId>` expiry), 2.8 (the artifact-key row and a new "Artifact key refusals" paragraph) and V27 ("B08 source authority", "Brain history restriction") are amended inline. The B08 row in `codium-track-b-checkpoint.md` replaces its "Redis/Lua remains CI-only" clause with BT03 and the remaining engine work. `v2-notes/at-vector-v2-plan.md` (section 3 V27, "Built") and `v2-notes/v2-complete-plan.md` (the V27 runtime row) gain a BT03 note with the engine-client rule.
- **Tests added** (all in `packages/web`, all picked up by the lane glob `test/cloud-agents-*.test.ts`):
  - `test/cloud-agents-brain.test.ts`, top level, so it runs with or without Redis: "every Redis key a Brain upload script touches is a declared KEYS entry, never an ARGV or built name" (S1; an index counts as declared only when a loop header binds it and no `local`, assignment or function parameter outside a loop header names it). Inside the Redis guard, the store wrapper gains a hook that can hold or rewrite one command, and six cases:
    - R1 "two concurrent commits of one upload store exactly one envelope, and a bound upload refuses another commit";
    - R2 "commit and read refuse an expired upload, including one that expires between the read and the atomic write";
    - R3 "the ciphertext and key wrap share one key whose expiry never grows past the upload TTL or the account index" (D1; failed at `ad3928f7`: `PTTL` 7,259,999 against 7,199,999; it ends with a key whose expiry was removed, which a read refuses with 503 and leaves at `PTTL` -1);
    - R4 "concurrent prepares never push an account past eight outstanding uploads";
    - R5 "account erasure racing a prepare or a commit leaves no upload, key wrap or index behind" (four cases: a prepare held while erasure runs; a prepare while erasure is pending, which the revocation mark that erasure needs first refuses, with the index erasure read unchanged and the held erasure still answering OK; a completed prepare; a commit held while erasure runs);
    - R6 "a read needs a committed, bound upload of the exact repository, generation and desktop source".
      Leftover checks filter the shared database by the fixture's own account (each value's `account`, and its own index key), and every expiry wait is bounded at 2 s.
  - `test/cloud-agents-start.test.ts` (real Redis; it reuses the file's fixture, which gains the desktop API, the artifact-key route, `post()` without the copy check for answers that carry random base64url, and a `brainRun()` helper: HTTP prepare, local AES-GCM encryption, commit and desktop start, then the bot's ticket mint):
    - E1 "an owner-started Brain run downloads exactly its approved bytes and records only immutable metadata";
    - E2 "a Brain run's key domain takes the repository's current visibility at its first download; a later change refuses" (both directions, each on a fresh run);
    - E3a "a Brain download needs the exact live desktop ticket, this repository's upload and a current allowance" (an ops ticket run and a writer's dispatch 503; an upload bound to another ticket, and another bound repository's upload bound to this run's ticket, 400; allowance off 503; then the same request with the allowance back 200);
    - E3b "a Brain download refuses a rebound, revoked, settled, expired or unreachable run and an expired upload" (401, 401, 401, 401 at the deadline, 503 on a store outage, 400 `BRAIN_UPLOAD_INVALID` when the upload's key has expired while the token is live);
    - E4 "an owner-desktop run never gets the ordinary conversation key, before or after its Brain download, while other ticket runs do" (D3; failed at `ad3928f7` with 200 and a key).
      Every refusal is checked for no `key` or `artifactKey` and no `brainSha` on any run record involved.
  - `test/cloud-agents-desktop.test.ts`: the fake store, fetch and limit count their calls, and the server routes by path. O1 "with the switch off every Brain route answers 503 before any method, body, credential, limit or store work": four routes × six requests (valid body, an extra field, a query string, `GET`, a foreign `Origin`, `text/plain`), all 503 `BRAIN_UPLOAD_UNAVAILABLE` with zero calls; switched on, the same prepare reaches one revocation fetch and stops at eligibility (D2; failed at `ad3928f7`, first at prepare `GET` 405).
  - `test/cloud-agents-owner-artifact.test.ts`: O2 "with the switch off the owner read answers 503 before any store or fetch" (`POST` and `GET`; passes at `ad3928f7` too, pinning the existing order).

### BT04 Engine Brain transport clients: artifact key and live-run Brain reader (2026-11-15, branch brain-transport)

- **What changed.** The engine side of the conversation-key and Brain transport, with nothing wired into the agent phase yet. Reasons, corrections and effects: deviations, "2026-11-15 BT04".
  - `packages/engine/src/cli/cmd/github.cloud.ts`:
    - `artifactKey({env, holder, services, signal})` posts exactly `{thread}` from `VECTOR_THREAD` to `ENDPOINT.artifactKey` with the holder's authorized run token (`runToken()`) and decodes a strict `{key, keyId}` (the shape `github.session.ts` `keyBytes` accepts). It sends nothing (`absent`) without an authorized valid token or a `THREAD_PATTERN` thread, and nothing (`brain-run`) when `VECTOR_BRAIN_UPLOAD` is non-empty (BT03 item 4, client half).
    - `brainDownload({env, holder, services, signal})` reads its id only from `env.VECTOR_BRAIN_UPLOAD` (no id parameter; deviations BT04 item 10), posts exactly `{uploadId}` to `ENDPOINT.brainDownload` and decodes the strict answer `{uploadId, key, aad, nonce, ciphertext, sha256, bytes, approvedAt, publicRepository, artifactKey?}`. The answer's `uploadId` must equal the one asked for. It sends nothing (`absent`) for an empty, missing or malformed id. The same env therefore makes `artifactKey` answer `brain-run`.
    - Both make one attempt with `redirect: "error"`, `credentials: "omit"` and `no-store`, mask every key, never throw or print, and answer `ok` or one of `absent`, `brain-run`, `refused`, `expired`, `not-allowed`, `unavailable` (`refused` and `expired` only for the route's own codes at their own status; `RUN_TOKEN_INVALID` is `refused`).
    - `body()` and `post()` take a response maximum, and `post()`'s signal, timeout and maximum are one options argument. Only `brainDownload` raises the cap, to 703,168 bytes; mint, bind, heartbeat and settle keep 16,384 bytes and their timeouts.
    - New exported types: `BrainDownload` and `KeyStatus`.
  - New `packages/engine/src/cli/cmd/github.brain.ts` (`GithubBrain.openUpload(download, {repositoryId, uploadId, publicRepository})`):
    - checks the `aad` as `packages/core/src/brain/upload.ts` does before encrypting (five parts, prefix, this repository id, this upload id, a positive generation, at most 256 characters), the repository id rule, the byte range and `approvedAt`;
    - decrypts with `node:crypto` AES-256-GCM, then requires the exact byte count and SHA-256, runs `BrainUpload.validate` with public when the server or the run's own event says so, and runs `BrainRunBundle.decode`;
    - returns `{bytes, bundle, approval: DesktopBrainApproval, brainKey?}`, or undefined on any refusal. It zeroes the decoded key, and the plaintext on refusal: the decipher's output is listed for zeroing before `final()` authenticates it, so a plaintext whose tag fails is zeroed too, and the result is that buffer itself with no copy. It makes no connection, so it needs no egress inventory entry.
  - Spec 2.8 (the `brain-download` row and a "Brain download" paragraph), 2.13 ("Conversation and Brain key clients") and the V27 Files list are amended inline. The B08 row in `codium-track-b-checkpoint.md`, `v2-notes/at-vector-v2-plan.md` (section 3 V27, items 1 and 3) and `v2-notes/v2-complete-plan.md` (the V27 runtime row) gain a BT04 note and the BT03 Valkey result.
- **Tests** (engine, picked up by the lane glob `test/cli/github*.test.ts`; neither file spawns a process):
  - `test/cli/github.cloud.test.ts`, new describe "conversation and Brain key clients" (30 tests, loopback fake service, the file's existing `fixture()`):
    - C1 "artifact key sends only the run's own thread and decodes a strict key" (an empty `VECTOR_BRAIN_UPLOAD` still asks);
    - C2, ten cases "artifact key <status> <code> is <result>": 400 `INVALID_REQUEST` and 401 `RUN_TOKEN_INVALID` refused, 401 `MODEL_PLAN_RUN_EXPIRED` expired, 403 `ARTIFACT_KEY_NOT_ALLOWED` not-allowed, 429 and three 503 codes unavailable, 403 `INVALID_REQUEST` (status mismatch) and 403 `ARTIFACT_KEY_NOT_ALLOWED` with `limit` unavailable. Each checks one request, no mask, no notice and no server text in the result;
    - C3 "malformed, oversized or redirected key answers grant nothing" (an extra field, a 42-character key, a noncanonical key, an uppercase key id, a 200 error body, an empty body, a key padded to 16,385 bytes; the same key padded to exactly 16,384 bytes is read; a 302 to a second loopback server, for both clients, which that server never receives);
    - C4 "a key or Brain read that never answers is cut off, one without a caller signal still has its own, and an aborted one never asks" (a caller signal of 100 ms cuts off a request that never answers; without a caller signal each client hands the transport an unaborted signal of its own; an already aborted signal sends nothing). No test waits out `post()`'s 20 s default, and mint's tests do not either, because mint always passes its own operation signal (deviations BT04 item 10);
    - C5 "own-key, unauthorized, threadless and Brain runs make no key request, and no id makes no Brain read" (an own-key holder, a loaded but unauthorized Codium holder and an empty holder for both clients; six bad threads; `VECTOR_BRAIN_UPLOAD` set to a valid or a malformed id answers `brain-run`; five bad upload ids and a step env without `VECTOR_BRAIN_UPLOAD`; 0 requests in all);
    - C6 "brain download sends only the step's own upload id, whose step never asks for the ordinary key, and decodes the largest approved envelope" (the step env that downloads makes `artifactKey` answer `brain-run` with no request; a 512 KiB upload, whose ciphertext is checked to be 699,072 characters, with and without `artifactKey`);
    - C7, fourteen cases "brain download <status> <code> is <result>" (400 `BRAIN_UPLOAD_INVALID` and 401 `RUN_TOKEN_INVALID` refused, 401 `MODEL_PLAN_RUN_EXPIRED` expired, and unavailable for 503 `BRAIN_UPLOAD_UNAVAILABLE`, `ABUSE_PROTECTION_UNAVAILABLE` and `RUN_ARTIFACTS_UNAVAILABLE`, 429, 403 `ORIGIN_NOT_ALLOWED`, 415, 413, 405, 400 `INVALID_JSON`, 403 `ARTIFACT_KEY_NOT_ALLOWED` and 401 `BRAIN_UPLOAD_INVALID`);
    - C8 "a Brain answer for another upload, with extra or out-of-range fields, or past its size cap grants nothing" (16 malformed answers, the largest answer padded to 703,169 bytes refused, and the same answer padded to exactly 703,168 bytes read). The test pins spec 2.13's literals 699,072 and 703,168 rather than recomputing the client's formula.

### BT05 V27 runtime save and runner attestation (2026-11-15, branch brain-transport)

- **What changed.** A Codium run with the runner's session proof now saves its conversation for the thread's next run. Reasons, corrections and effects: deviations, "2026-11-15 BT05".
  - `packages/engine/src/cli/cmd/github.agent.ts`:
    - `record()` (`:611`) calls `saveConversation` after the share step and before `writeBundle`, Codium only, unless a signal has ended the run.
    - `saveConversation` (`:641`) removes any `vector-out/session.vsa` inside a real directory, then returns with no line on a Brain run (`VECTOR_BRAIN_UPLOAD`), without a session, or when `sessionProof()` is false. `writeConversation` (`:668`) checks the identity (`conversationIdentity`, `:701`: `GITHUB_REPOSITORY_ID`, the route's thread, mint's `VECTOR_RUN_ID` for this repository, run and attempt), exports the root session, refuses an owner-only one, redacts it, skips one over `PATCH_LIMITS.sessionBytes - SESSION_HEADROOM` (16,384 bytes, exported, `:88`) before any key request, asks `artifactKey` once, encrypts, writes `session.vsa` exclusively with mode 0600 into a real `vector-out/` only and attests the bytes. Anything but "saved" removes the file; an abort logs nothing; otherwise one fixed line.
    - New exported lines `SESSION_SAVED`, `SESSION_NOT_SAVED`, `SESSION_TOO_LARGE` (`:79-83`) and `SESSION_HEADROOM` (`:88`); types `SessionExport` (`:113`) and `SessionSave` (`:122`); `AgentPhaseInput.session` (`:142`); `AgentCredentials` adds `sessionProof` (`:109`).
    - Defaults (`:210-221`): on Codium only, the export is `PublicSessionShare`'s `export` (looked up with `Effect.serviceOption`, never `publish` or `flush`), schema-encoded by `portableArchive` (`:710`); the codec is `encryptSession` (`:717`, a dynamic import of `github.session.ts`); the attestation is `GithubSessionProof.attestSessionArtifact`.
  - `packages/engine/src/cli/cmd/github.cloud.ts`: `MINTED_RUN_ID` (`:13`) is exported and used by mint's schema, so the save and mint share one run-id pattern.
  - `packages/engine/test/cli/github.agent-fixture.ts`: `REPOSITORY_ID`, `RID` and `ARTIFACT_KEY`; `cloudRecorder` (`:225`) answers artifact-key requests with an optional `artifactKey` (only that path) and returns `masks` and `notices`; `holder` (`:266`) takes `{sessionProof}`, which loads this build's tool boundary and the FD3 name. Defaults are unchanged.

Each: outcome `changes`, only `patch.diff` and `summary.json`, `NOTES.md` in the patch, no stop, no attestation, one `SESSION_NOT_SAVED`, one key request then settle, P6 clean.

  - S8 "without a proof channel, an identity of this run, a session archive or a shareable session, no key is asked": nine rows (no proof channel, which logs nothing; no `VECTOR_RUN_ID`; another repository's, attempt's or run's rid; no `GITHUB_REPOSITORY_ID`; a failed export; an owner-only session; no session archive in the runtime), each `SESSION_NOT_SAVED` except the first, settle only, no attestation, no file, P6 clean.

The evidence body leaves out restored messages (`:616`).

### BT08b V30b engine journey: desktop Brain run end to end (2026-11-16, branch brain-transport)

- **What changed.** The engine journey now runs a desktop Brain run end to end with fakes only: Vector's App-bot ticket dispatch of the generated file with `brain_upload`, route (`dispatch-<runId>`), mint, one brain-download answering bytes sealed by core's desktop encryption beside the Brain key the server derives, the agent with the labelled Brain context, `session.vsa` under the Brain key with the approved bundle, and publish against the fake GitHub and the bare remote. Building it found a product bug, fixed in its owning file: the v3 publish step never received the dispatch prompt, so the publisher refused every dispatched task after the agent had done its work. ### BT09 V30c server journey: artifact keys and the Brain transport (2026-11-16, branch brain-transport)

- **What changed.** The server journey now drives the V27 and B08 routes end to end over real Redis, next to its existing bind-to-rebind case, and building it found one product defect, fixed in its owning file: the owner read's run-record lookup refused every record when the store client speaks RESP3. Reasons, corrections and effects: deviations, "2026-11-16 BT09".
  - `api/_lib/cloud-agents-runs.ts` (owned, spec 0.2): `ownerCloudRun` (`:247-250`) reads the run hash with `HGETALL` inside a one-line script (`RECORD`, `:448-450`) instead of a bare `HGETALL`, so the reply is a flat array whatever protocol the store client speaks, as `cloud-agents-binding.ts` READ already does and says. Before the fix, a RESP3 client's map made `fields` refuse the record (503 `PERSISTENT_STORE_UNAVAILABLE`), so `owner-artifact-key` answered 503 `ARTIFACT_READ_UNAVAILABLE` for every completed desktop run on this lane's Valkey and local Redis (deviations item 1).
  - `packages/web/test/cloud-agents-runs.test.ts` (`:452`): new "a completed desktop run's owner provenance reads its record through a RESP3 store client" (real Redis, through the file's RESP3 bridge; the flat-array reply a REST store sends is covered by `cloud-agents-owner-artifact.test.ts`'s fake store): a live desktop dispatch record is `RUN_UNAVAILABLE` until it ends, then `ownerCloudRun` returns exactly its account, rid, repository, generation, thread, run id, start and end; another account gets `RUN_UNAVAILABLE`. It fails before the fix with "Run records are temporarily unavailable." (`red-runs.log`).
  - `packages/web/test/cloud-agents-owner-artifact.test.ts` (`:77-96`): the fake store now answers the run record for the scripted read (`EVAL`, the exact `HGETALL` script, one key) instead of a bare `HGETALL`; its six cases are otherwise unchanged.
  - `packages/web/test/cloud-agents-journey.test.ts` (239 to 1036 lines):
    - `stage()` (`:104`): one repository behind its own signed fake GitHub App (`github-app-fixture.ts`), served by the real handlers on a loopback `node:http` server under the names of their `api/cloud-agents/*.ts` files (`bind-code`, `bind`, `mint`, `heartbeat`, `settle`, `runs`, `artifact-key`, `brain-prepare`, `brain-commit`, `brain-download`, `desktop-run`, `owner-artifact-key`). `createCloudAgentsApi` with the fixture's App and verifier, `createDesktopCloudAgentApi` with the real default limit and `prepareCloudRun` (entitlement from the fixture), and `createOwnerArtifactReadApi` with its real limit. The desktop routes and the owner read take `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` only from the stage's env object, a getter over `policy.brain`; `eligible` is a plain fixture flag standing for Codium's answer (deviations items 4 and 5). Helpers: `send` (the three safe headers on every answer, every body kept for the P6 scan), `ownerToken` (marked desktop) and `cliToken` (unmarked), `bind` (`:207`), `mint` (`:228`, a person's comment run on thread 17 or the App bot's ticket dispatch with the start test's proven claims), `upload` (`:263`, core's `BrainRunBundle.pack` and `BrainUpload.upload` through the real prepare and commit routes, with a lesson, a shareable skill that travels only when the fixture repository is private, and one insight), `desktopRun` (`:344`) and `client` (`:357`, the engine's `Services`: only `https://vectordev.ai/api/cloud-agents/*` is accepted and rewritten to the stage's server; no identity token, no notice). with the fixture's answer off the download is 503 and records nothing; the engine's `brainDownload` reads the real answer (`status: "ok"`), the route's own answer is the same object with exactly the ten documented keys, the content key and `aad` are the prepare answer's, `publicRepository` is the repository's visibility, the Brain key is `cloudBrainArtifactKey` for the run's scope, approved digest and visibility (not the ordinary key, and not the other visibility's key), `brainSha` and `brainPublic` (`1` public, `0` private) are recorded, both keys are masked, and the engine's `GithubBrain.openUpload` returns the packed bytes, the lesson, the skill only for the private repository, the approval with its visibility and the Brain key; heartbeat, the static verifier and `requireModelPlanCaller` agree; settle records `changes`/`pass` with the desktop trigger and no provenance fields; after settle the download (route and engine client) and artifact-key are 401. The owner read for the completed run returns exactly the Brain key, repository, thread, rid, artifact id and visibility (`publicRepository` false for the private repository), minting `{metadata: read}` then `{actions: read, metadata: read}` (both revoked) and fetching no archive; it refuses a writer who lost write access (fresh check), a wrong artifact name, an expired artifact, another workflow run and a non-dispatch run, each alone, and a body thread, a run token or an unmarked token before any GitHub request; a same-account rebind ends it. The P6 scan holds and no App token is left.

### BT12 V31 Ship Desk capture job (2026-11-16, branch brain-transport)

- **What changed.** Codium workflows now ask for Ship Desk's capture ticket in the agent job, right after the mint and before checkout, and run Ship Desk's **Vector capture** job on the pull request publish made, only with `vars.VECTOR_SHIP_DESK == '1'`. Own-key files are byte-identical. `origin/codium-finish` and `origin/main` carry no capture-job commit (`git grep "VECTOR_SHIP_DESK\|ship ticket"` of `workflow.ts` on both is empty). Reasons, corrections and effects: deviations, "2026-11-16 BT12".
  - `packages/core/src/github-agents.ts` (constants the hosted API imports): `JOB.capture = "Vector capture"` (Codium only; no `id-token`, so the App's job check never sees it as a profile) and `TIMEOUT_MIN.capture = 15` (item 6).
  - `packages/core/src/review/workflow.ts` (owned): `WorkflowStep` gains `"timeout-minutes"` (unexported); the Codium agent job drops its npm cache restore and gains the ticket step `{id: "capture", if: "steps.mint.outputs.status == 'ok' && vars.VECTOR_SHIP_DESK == '1'", run: "vector ship ticket >/dev/null 2>&1", shell: <the agent step's>, "continue-on-error": true, "timeout-minutes": 2}` between mint and checkout, and the output `capture: ${{ steps.capture.outputs.capture }}`; the Codium review job drops its restore; the Codium publish job gains `outputs: {pr: ${{ steps.publish.outputs.pr }}}` and its publish step `id: publish`; the new Codium-only `capture` job (between publish and bind): needs route, agent and publish, `if: !cancelled() && vars.VECTOR_SHIP_DESK == '1' && needs.agent.outputs.capture != '' && needs.publish.outputs.pr != ''`, `contents: read` only, 15 minutes, env `{VECTOR_WORKFLOW_VERSION}`, steps setup-node, Install Vector (no cache), checkout of `refs/pull/<pr>/head` (`fetch-depth: 0`, `persist-credentials: false`), `vector ship capture --pr "$VECTOR_CAPTURE_PR"` with `VECTOR_CAPTURE_PR` and `VECTOR_CAPTURE_TICKET` in env (items 1 to 3, 5, 7). `buildAgentsWorkflow`'s signature and exports are unchanged; no `WORKFLOW_VERSION` bump (item 8).
